# gjsify — Project Status

> **STATUS.md is a CURRENT SNAPSHOT, not a changelog.** It records the present state of each
> pillar/package, the metrics, the integration coverage, and the open work — kept current *in
> place*, never appended to. Per-change history lives where it belongs: **git commit messages**
> (the detailed narrative, co-located with the diff — `git log`/`git blame`) and **CHANGELOG.md**
> (what shipped, when). Do **not** add dated `Current state (…)` log blocks here.
>
> *(The old append-only "Current state" log + `### Completed` done-log were removed 2026-06-19:
> they duplicated git/CHANGELOG, ballooned to ~940 KB nobody read end-to-end, taxed every PR, and
> got silently corrupted by a blunt doc-version bump. Full history remains in `git log -p STATUS.md`.)*

## Release timeline

- **v0.7.2** — `@gjsify/module` PnP-aware `createRequire`. Parses `.pnp.cjs`'s `RAW_RUNTIME_STATE` directly (no Yarn-manifest execution), so `require.resolve("<pkg>/package.json")` works in PnP-built bundles for the first time. Removed the last reason ts-for-gir had to embed its EJS templates at build time.
- **v0.3.12** — `cli,esbuild-plugin-gjsify` library multi-build fix: `format`/`jsExtension` were being passed the wrong sibling outdir/extension, so the ESM and CJS halves of a `--library` build collided in the CJS directory. New e2e suite: `library-multi-build/`.
- **v0.3.11** — Three rewriter fixes that let PnP-built bundles run portably without `nodeLinker: node-modules`: (1) banner-shebang hoist keeps `#!/usr/bin/env -S gjs -m` at byte 0 even when esbuild's own banner runs first; (2) `createRequire(<URL>)` of zip-resident anchors becomes a thrown-on-use stub (esbuild has already inlined every dep — the createRequire result is dead code in our bundles); (3) `import.meta.url` URL rewrite is skipped for source files inside Yarn-PnP virtual zips so the bare token resolves to the bundle's own URL at runtime.
- **v0.3.10** — Static-read inliner: build-time evaluation of `readFileSync` / `readdirSync` / `existsSync` / `JSON.parse(readFileSync(...))` against `import.meta.url`-relative URLs, replaces calls with literal contents → bundle is portable to any directory. New e2e suite: `inline-static-reads/` (1/1).
- **v0.3.9** — Config precedence + outfile safety + PnP-zip rewriter warning. `package.json#gjsify` now merges with `.gjsifyrc.*`/`gjsify.config.*` (was first-match-wins). `gjsify build` refuses to default `--outfile` to a `.ts` source path. New e2e suite: `cli-config/` (2/2).
- **v0.3.8** — Native install backend default. `@gjsify/semver` (77/77), `@gjsify/npm-registry` (33/33), `@gjsify/tar` (27/25 Node/GJS) compose into `install-backend-native.ts` — BFS resolver, parallel tarball download with sha512 SRI, flat `node_modules/` + `.bin/` symlinks. New e2e suite: `native-install/`.
- **v0.3.7** — PnP-rewriter onLoad ordering fix: the `__filename`/`__dirname` rewriter is composed INTO the pnp plugin's onLoad (esbuild stops at the first match). Extracted `getPnpPlugin` from `@gjsify/cli` into reusable `@gjsify/resolve-npm/pnp-relay` with a `transformContentsFactory` callback. New e2e suite: `cli-only-pnp/` (5/5).
- **v0.3.6** — pnpapi namespace unwrap, esbuild peer dep, lazy-deepkit. `cli-only-pnp` suite (4/4).
- **v0.3.5** — `gjsify dlx` GJS-bundle runner; PnP zip read; two-hop relay shape; shebang config; `@gjsify/webassembly` Promise-API polyfill (15 tests).
- **v0.3.0–0.3.4** — Yarn PnP support, `excludeGlobals`, fetch bridge-free, UNDECLARED_DEPENDENCY fall-through, portable `import.meta.url`.

## Summary

gjsify implements Node.js, Web Standard, and DOM APIs for GJS (GNOME JavaScript / SpiderMonkey 140).
The project comprises **41 Node.js modules** (+2 meta, +5 native bridges), **19 Web API packages** (+1 meta, +1 native bridge, +5 Adwaita packages), **2 DOM packages**, **6 framework bridge packages** (+1 app-shell package), **3 GJS infrastructure packages**, and **21 build/infra tools**.

| Category | Total | Full | Partial | Stub |
|----------|-------|------|---------|------|
| Node.js APIs | 41 | 33 (80%) | 5 (12%) | 3 (7%) |
| Node.js native bridges | 5 | 5 (terminal, sab, tls, http-soup, http2) | — | — |
| Node.js meta | 2 | 2 (node-polyfills, browser-node-polyfills) | — | — |
| Web APIs | 19 | 17 (89%) | 2 (11%) | — |
| Web native bridge | 1 | 1 (webrtc-native) | — | — |
| Web meta | 1 | 1 | — | — |
| Browser UI / Adwaita | 5 | 5 (adwaita-web, adwaita-fonts, adwaita-icons, adwaita-core, adwaita-storybook) | — | — |
| DOM | 2 | 2 (dom-elements, canvas2d-core) | — | — |
| Framework bridges | 6 | 6 (bridge-types, canvas2d, event-bridge, iframe, video, webgl) | — | — |
| Framework storybook | 3 | 3 (stories, storybook, storybook-core) | — | — |
| Framework app shell | 1 | 1 (adwaita-app — app base + nav shell + async mounter + `LoadingStack` widget + storybook) | — | — |
| GJS Infrastructure | 3 | 3 (runtime, unit, utils) | — | — |
| Build/Infra Tools | 21 | 21 | — | — |
| Showcases | 8 | 8 | — | — |
| Integration test suites | 35 | 35 | — | — |

**Web platform coverage** (vs. the relevant W3C/WHATWG standards, not just our own package list): ≈54 % of all surveyed standards implemented full or partial, with ~20 % out of scope by design for desktop GTK apps (Service Worker, FS Access, Web Bluetooth, …). See `website/src/data/web-standards.ts` for the canonical category list.

**Test coverage:** 10,650+ test cases in 114+ spec files (each test runs on both Node.js and GJS). (86 new `@gjsify/child_process` parity tests in v0.7.2's audit.) CI via GitHub Actions (Node.js 24.x + GJS on Fedora 43/44 — minimum supported runtime: GJS 1.86 / SpiderMonkey 140). Integration suites (`gjsify foreach test:integration`) are opt-in and exercise curated upstream tests from acorn, autobahn, axios, chalk, chokidar, claude-agent-sdk, cosmiconfig, debug, deepkit-type-compiler, deltachat, devtools-cdp, dotenv, execa, fast-glob, gettext-parser, lightningcss, loro-crdt, mcp-inspector-cli, mcp-typescript-sdk, minify-xml, nativescript, oxfmt-native, pkg-types, rolldown-native, rollup-pluginutils, socket.io, streamx, tls-session, ts-for-gir, typescript-tsc, undici, webtorrent, worker-stress, yargs, and yjs.

## Package Tiers

Every published package declares its stability contract in `package.json#gjsify.tier` — **the source of truth**, verified by `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs --check` in CI (tier presence + dependency direction: `dependencies`/`optionalDependencies` must point to the same or a lower tier — devDeps/optional peers are the sanctioned seams — and no Tier-1/2 package may hard-depend on `@gjsify/node-gi`). See [ADR 0003](docs/adr/0003-package-tiering.md) + [ADR 0005](docs/adr/0005-node-gi-scope.md).

- **Tier 1 — core (99):** stability promise — full dual-runtime CI, root-cause governance, no known-broken releases. All `packages/node/*` (48, native bridges included), all `packages/web/*` except the `adwaita-*` packages (21), `packages/dom/*` (2), `packages/gjs/*` (3), the framework bridges `bridge-types`/`canvas2d`/`event-bridge`/`iframe`/`video`/`webgl` (6), and all `packages/infra/*` except `nativescript-vite` (19).
- **Tier 2 — product (27):** best effort — tested, released on the train, but breaking changes may ship with a minor + changelog note. Design identity (`adwaita-web`, `adwaita-fonts`, `adwaita-icons`, `adwaita-core`, `adwaita-nativescript`), storybook (`stories`, `storybook-core`, `storybook`, `adwaita-storybook`, `storybook-nativescript`), the native app shell (`adwaita-app`), devtools (`devtools`, `devtools-protocol`, `devtools-nativescript`, `devtools-mcp` — the MCP bridge; its Tier-3 CDP profile is a lazy-loaded optional peer, so its walked deps stay Tier ≤ 2), the NativeScript bridges (`native-platform`, `native-fs-bridge`), `nativescript-vite`, the 8 published showcases (`@gjsify/example-*`), and **`node-gi`** (the Axis-5 reverse bridge — graduated Tier 3→2 on 2026-07-14 per ADR 0005; the dependency-isolation invariant is retained, so it stays a devDep-only seam).
- **Tier 3 — experimental (3):** no promise; new axes start here. `devtools-browser`, `devtools-cdp`, and **`napi`** (the N-API-host-in-GJS axis — the forward mirror of `node-gi`; Phase 0, NOT a workspace member, own `napi.yml` CI).

### Native Bridges

| Package | GNOME Libs | Description |
|---------|-----------|-------------|
| **@gjsify/terminal-native** (Vala) | Linux/Posix (Vala VAPIs) | Optional Vala prebuild providing real Linux terminal syscalls to `@gjsify/tty` and `@gjsify/process`. `Terminal` class: `is_tty(fd)` → `Posix.isatty()`, `get_size(fd, out rows, out cols, out xpixel, out ypixel)` → `ioctl(TIOCGWINSZ)`, `set_raw_mode(fd, enable)` → `tcgetattr/tcsetattr` (clears ICANON+ECHO+ISIG — Node-parity raw mode, Ctrl-C delivered as `\x03`). `ResizeWatcher` class: `resized(rows, cols)` GObject signal wired to SIGWINCH via `GLib.Unix.signal_add()`. TypeScript wrapper: synchronous `imports.gi.GjsifyTerminal` with try/catch — safe when typelib not installed, no crash. Consumers see native behaviour when installed, GLib/env fallback otherwise. Ships as `.so`+`.typelib` in `prebuilds/linux-x64/`. CI builds pending (add to `.github/workflows/prebuilds.yml`). |
| **@gjsify/http-soup-bridge** (Vala) | Soup 3.0 | Vala/GObject library consumed by `@gjsify/http`. Wraps `Soup.Server` + `SoupServerMessage` and exposes JS only through plain GObject classes whose lifetime SpiderMonkey GC cannot race against. Solves two libsoup GC crashes: (1) `BoxedBase::finalize → g_source_unref` SIGSEGV from deferred-GC on in-flight Soup messages, (2) `g_main_context_unref` assertion from shared `GMainContext` ref imbalance. Contains `Server` (wraps `Soup.Server`, emits `request-received` / `upgrade` / `error-occurred` signals), `Request` (read-side snapshot — method, url, headers, `get_body()`), `Response` (write side, owns `SoupServerMessage` C-side including all pause/unpause bookkeeping), and a peer-close watcher (`g_socket_create_source(IN|HUP|ERR)` + non-blocking `MSG_PEEK` probe — capability unreachable from JS because `Gio.Socket.receive_message` is not introspectable). Ships as prebuilt `.so` + `.typelib` in `prebuilds/linux-{x64,arm64,ppc64,s390x,riscv64}/`; CI (`.github/workflows/prebuilds.yml`) rebuilds on Vala source changes (native runners for x64/arm64; QEMU via `uraimo/run-on-arch-action` for ppc64/s390x/riscv64). |
| **@gjsify/http2-native** (Vala + C shim) | libnghttp2 ≥ 1.40 | Vala/GObject library consumed by `@gjsify/http2`. Provides nghttp2 primitives unreachable through libsoup's high-level GIR API. Contains `FrameEncoder` (HPACK header-block encoder + raw frame builders for DATA / HEADERS / PUSH_PROMISE / SETTINGS / WINDOW_UPDATE / PING / RST_STREAM / GOAWAY — backed by a tiny C shim around `nghttp2_hd_deflate_*` since nghttp2 has no upstream Vala VAPI), `StreamIdAllocator` (server-side even-id sequencer per RFC 7540 §5.1.1, plus client-id tracking for GOAWAY), and **`SessionBridge`** (full `nghttp2_session` driver — server + client modes via `nghttp2_session_{server,client}_new`, `mem_recv`/`mem_send`, complete submit family (`submit_settings/response/request/data/push_promise/goaway/rst_stream`); per-stream HEADERS accumulator + GLib.Bytes-owned DATA carrier; events drain through `gjsify_http2_event_*` getters and re-emit on the main loop via `GLib.Idle.add()` — same hop pattern as `@gjsify/webrtc-native`). Header signals carry `GLib.Variant("a(ss)")` instead of `string[]` so GJS's marshaller doesn't trip the `G_VALUE_HOLDS_POINTER` assertion. All buffer ownership stays C-side via `GLib.Bytes` so SpiderMonkey GC cannot race nghttp2 allocations — same pattern as `@gjsify/webrtc-native` / `@gjsify/http-soup-bridge`. TS wrapper loads the typelib lazily via try/catch — packages fall back to JS-only counters when the prebuild is unavailable. Ships as `.so` + `.typelib` in `prebuilds/linux-{x64,arm64,ppc64,s390x,riscv64}/`; CI (`.github/workflows/prebuilds.yml`) rebuilds on Vala source changes. |
| **@gjsify/sab-native** (Vala + C shim) | Linux libc (memfd_create, mmap, futex, socketpair, SCM_RIGHTS) | Vala/GObject library providing cross-process shared memory + atomics for `@gjsify/worker_threads`. Closes the SharedArrayBuffer gap in stock GJS (Mozilla disables the SAB constructor without COOP/COEP opts; even with SAB on, subprocess workers cannot share backing stores by design — SM allocates SABs from the per-runtime malloc heap). Two GObject classes: **`SharedBuffer`** (wraps a `memfd_create(2)` + `mmap(MAP_SHARED)` region, exposes typed accessors `get_u8/u32_le/i32_le/u64_le` + write counterparts, bulk `read_bytes/write_bytes` via `GLib.Bytes` with refcount-pinned mmap lifetime, SEQ_CST atomics `atomic_{add,sub,load,store,xchg,cmpxchg}_i32` via GCC `__atomic_*` builtins, and `futex_wait/futex_wake` via raw `syscall(SYS_futex, FUTEX_{WAIT,WAKE}_PRIVATE)` for cross-process wait/notify) + **`FdChannel`** (Unix-domain `SOCK_SEQPACKET` socketpair + `sendmsg/recvmsg` with `SCM_RIGHTS` for fd-transfer; SOCK_CLOEXEC on both ends; 4-byte big-endian tag payload mapping each fd to the JSON-side placeholder index). Two construction paths: `SharedBuffer.create(size)` allocates fresh anonymous memory; `SharedBuffer.from_fd(fd, size)` mmaps a fd received over `SCM_RIGHTS`. Fd dup'd on `from_fd` so sender can close their copy. Wire protocol is byte-order-explicit LE across all multi-byte accessors so a buffer written on x86_64 reads identically on s390x. TS façade (`SharedBuffer`, `atomics` namespace, internal `fdChannel`) loads typelib via synchronous `imports.gi.GjsifySabNative` with try/catch — `hasNativeSab()` predicate. 18 GJS specs green including a real `Gio.SubprocessLauncher.take_fd(fd, 3)` cross-process round-trip + SCM_RIGHTS fd transfer round-trip. Ships as `.so` + `.gir` + `.typelib` in `prebuilds/linux-{x64,arm64,ppc64,s390x,riscv64}/`. |
| **@gjsify/tls-native** (Vala) | GnuTLS 3.x (`gnutls_ocsp_*`) | Optional Vala prebuild providing GnuTLS capabilities not exposed by `Gio.TlsConnection`. **Phase 1 (this release): OCSP-response parsing.** Vala's bundled `gnutls.vapi` (0.56) covers the core handshake / X.509 / pubkey surface but has no `gnutls_ocsp_*` declarations — `@gjsify/tls-native` ships a sibling `gnutls-ocsp.vapi` declaring the minimal OCSP surface and a `Tls.parse_ocsp_response(uint8[])` static method that returns an `OcspResponseInfo` GObject (response status, producedAt, certStatus, thisUpdate, nextUpdate, revocationTime, revocationReason — all RFC 6960 §4.2.1 fields). The split `.vapi` (declarations) + `.vala` (impl) avoids the duplicate `typedef gnutls_xxx_t` collision that hits when the `cname = "struct gnutls_xxx_int"` pattern appears in a `.vala` source. TS wrapper: `hasNativeTls()` predicate, `parseOcspResponse(bytes): OcspResponseInfo \| null`, `OcspCertStatus` / `OcspResponseStatus` symbolic enum-style constants. 8 GJS specs green (typelib loads, parser rejects empty/garbage/truncated input, symbolic constants match RFC values). Builds against `gnutls-devel` (Fedora) / `libgnutls28-dev` (Debian). Phase 2 candidates (session resumption + channel binding) still blocked on extracting the live `gnutls_session_t` from a running `Gio.TlsConnection`. Ships as `.so` + `.gir` + `.typelib` in `prebuilds/linux-x64/` (multi-arch via CI to follow). |

Aliases: npm `ws` and `isomorphic-ws` both resolve here. | `ping`/`pong` events (Soup handles control frames internally — libsoup 3 GI does not expose user-level send API), `upgrade`/`unexpected-response`/`redirect` events (no Soup hook) |
| **worker_threads** | Gio, GLib | 288 (gjs) / 281 (node) + integration suite (1034 gjs / 1169 node) | MessageChannel, MessagePort (deep clone: Date, RegExp, Map, Set, Error, TypedArrays), BroadcastChannel, receiveMessageOnPort, environmentData, Worker (Gio.Subprocess with stdin/stdout IPC, **file-based resolution with relative paths**, missing-file error handling, stderr capture, **orphan shutdown** — EOF on the child's stdin means the spawner is gone, so the bootstrap exits the process outright instead of quitting a main loop it has not entered yet, which previously stranded a worker whose script was still running as a permanent 100%-CPU orphan), **addEventListener/removeEventListener on MessagePort/BroadcastChannel**, structured clone edge cases (-0, NaN, BigInt, Int32Array), **`postMessage(value, transferList)` for `ArrayBuffer`** (zero-copy via SM140 `ArrayBuffer.prototype.transfer()` — sender detaches; multiple buffers per call; rejects detached / duplicate / non-transferable / SAB entries with `DataCloneError`/code 25), **`postMessage(value, transferList)` for `MessagePort`** (in-process channel hand-off — source port detaches, receiver gets a usable port wired to the surviving channel partner; supports nested transfer chains, rejects self-transfer / closed-port transfer), **`SharedArrayBuffer` pass-through in same-process MessageChannel** (Node only — same backing store; sender writes visible to receiver via `Atomics.{load,add}`), **cross-process `SharedBuffer` in `Worker.postMessage` and `workerData`** (via [`@gjsify/sab-native`](#native-bridges): parent's `FdChannel.makePair()` at spawn passes the child end as inherited fd 3 via `Gio.SubprocessLauncher.take_fd`; each `postMessage` walks the value tree for `SharedBuffer` instances and SCM_RIGHTS-sends their fds tagged with a sequence counter before writing the JSON line with `{__sab: tag, size: N}` placeholders; the bootstrap counts placeholders per inbound message and synchronously drains exactly that many fds from fd 3 — kernel buffering guarantees the fds are ready since the parent always writes them before the stdin line — then rebuilds each placeholder via `SharedBuffer.fromFd(fd, size)`) | `SharedArrayBuffer` constructor unavailable in stock GJS (Mozilla disables it; use `SharedBuffer` instead), `worker.postMessage` IPC-side `ArrayBuffer` transferList (Gio.Subprocess JSON protocol — no binary frames yet for non-SharedBuffer transfers) |

## Web API Packages (`packages/web/`)

All 20 packages have real implementations (plus 1 meta). New in this cycle: `@gjsify/xmlhttprequest` (split out of fetch), `@gjsify/domparser` (excalibur-tiled), `@gjsify/webrtc`, `@gjsify/webrtc-native`, `@gjsify/adwaita-fonts`, `@gjsify/adwaita-icons`, `@gjsify/web-polyfills`, `@gjsify/webassembly` (Promise-API polyfill).

| Package | GNOME Libs | Tests | Web APIs |
|---------|-----------|-------|----------|
| **abort-controller** | — | 23 (2 specs) | AbortController, AbortSignal (.abort, .timeout, .any) |
| **compression-streams** | — | 29 | CompressionStream, DecompressionStream (gzip/deflate/deflate-raw). Uses @gjsify/web-streams TransformStream |
| **dom-events** | — | 292 gjs / 290 node (4 specs) | Event, EventTarget, CustomEvent; `dispatchEvent` reports a throwing listener and keeps going (WHATWG DOM § 2.7) — the report step is itself guarded, and the dispatch flags are restored in a `finally` so a hostile throw cannot wedge the event |
| **dom-exception** | — | 64 | DOMException polyfill (WebIDL standard) |
| **domparser** | — | 23 | DOMParser (parseFromString XML + HTML), minimal DOM (Element tagName/getAttribute/children/childNodes/querySelector[All]/textContent/innerHTML, Document documentElement/querySelector[All]). Sized for excalibur-tiled map parsing |
| **eventsource** | — | 15 | EventSource (Server-Sent Events), TextLineStream. Uses fetch + Web Streams |
| **fetch** | Soup 3.0, Gio, GLib | 103 | fetch(), Request, Response, Headers, Referrer-Policy, **file:// URI support**. Raw request body via `set_request_body_from_bytes` (fixes POST body never sent). **Per-connection TLS opt-out** (`rejectUnauthorized: false`, undici-style) accepting the peer cert on **that `Soup.Message` only** — never the shared session, so other fetches keep verifying — plus process-global `NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0` (explicit option wins); **AbortSignal honored during connect/request-send/TTFB** (abort→cancel wired before `send_async`; a cancelled send surfaces as `AbortError`, Node parity — closes the hung-server timeout gap). XHR + `URL.createObjectURL` moved out into `@gjsify/xmlhttprequest` + `@gjsify/url` |
| **xmlhttprequest** | Soup 3.0, GLib | covered via fetch `on('Gjs', …)` | XMLHttpRequest (full `responseType`: arraybuffer / blob + temp-file / json / text / document). FakeBlob with `_tmpPath`. `URL.createObjectURL`/`revokeObjectURL` are first-class methods on `@gjsify/url`'s URL class — this package just owns the blob-file plumbing and the XHR class |
| **formdata** | — | 49 | FormData, File, multipart encoding |
| **streams** | — | 345 | ReadableStream (incl. **`type:'bytes'`**), WritableStream, TransformStream, **ReadableByteStreamController** + **`autoAllocateChunkSize`**, **ReadableStreamBYOBReader** (`read(view, {min?})` preserves view-constructor), **ReadableStreamBYOBRequest** (`view`/`respond`/`respondWithNewView`), polymorphic close/error/cancel for default + BYOB readers, byte-stream `tee()`, TextEncoderStream, TextDecoderStream, ByteLengthQueuingStrategy, CountQueuingStrategy (WHATWG Streams polyfill for GJS) |
| **webcrypto** | Gio (via the `/random` chain), GLib | 508 | SubtleCrypto (digest, AES-CBC/CTR/GCM, AES-KW (RFC 3394), HMAC, ECDSA, RSA-PSS, RSA-OAEP, PBKDF2, HKDF, ECDH, generateKey, importKey/exportKey, deriveBits/deriveKey, wrapKey/unwrapKey), CryptoKey. **Owns the workspace entropy chain** — `@gjsify/webcrypto/random` (leaf subpath, no SubtleCrypto/DOMException, consumed by `@gjsify/crypto` without a cycle): `fillRandomBytes()` returns the tier it reached (WebCrypto → `/dev/urandom` via Gio → `GLib.random_int_range` → `Math.random`), warns once when it has to fall below a CSPRNG, and takes `{webcrypto: null}` so the polyfill can use the chain without recursing into itself |
| **web-globals** | — | 66 | Unified re-export surface for all Web API packages. Root export is pure named re-exports; side effects (registering URL, URLSearchParams, Blob, File, FormData, performance, PerformanceObserver + chaining every sub-package's `/register`) live in `@gjsify/web-globals/register`. Users opt in via the `--globals` CLI flag or an explicit `import '@gjsify/web-globals/register'`. |
| **websocket** | Soup 3.0, Gio, GLib | 27 | WebSocket, MessageEvent, CloseEvent (W3C spec) |
| **webaudio** | Gst 1.0, GstApp 1.0, Gio | 35 | AudioContext (decodeAudioData via GStreamer decodebin, createBufferSource, createGain, currentTime via GLib monotonic clock), AudioBuffer (PCM Float32Array storage), AudioBufferSourceNode (GStreamer appsrc→audioconvert→volume→autoaudiosink), GainNode (AudioParam with setTargetAtTime), AudioParam, HTMLAudioElement (canPlayType, playbin playback). **Phase 1 — covers Excalibur.js.** Guaranteed NULL-state teardown (`gst-teardown.ts`): every pipeline is tracked and drained via `GApplication::shutdown` / `process.on('exit')` / `AudioContext.close()` / a `finally` in the decoder, so none is finalized in PLAYING (one GStreamer-CRITICAL per element). **No runtime branching:** decode + playback run on gjs AND on the node-gi reverse bridge (node/bun/deno) — the former bun/deno refusal is gone, see the Axis-5 entry under `## Open TODOs` |
| **gamepad** | Manette 0.2 | 19 | Gamepad (navigator.getGamepads polling via libmanette event-driven signals), GamepadButton (pressed/touched/value), GamepadEvent (gamepadconnected/gamepaddisconnected on globalThis), GamepadHapticActuator (dual-rumble with strong/weak magnitude). Button mapping: Manette→W3C standard layout (17 buttons incl. triggers-as-buttons). Axis mapping: 4 stick axes + trigger axes→button values. Lazy Manette.Monitor init, graceful degradation without libmanette. |
| **webrtc** | Gst 1.0, GstWebRTC 1.0, GstSDP 1.0 | 307 (4 specs; 1 red — upstream GStreamer empty-message defect, see the package section) | **Phase 1–4 — Data Channel + Media + Stats & Advanced.** RTCPeerConnection (offer/answer, ICE trickle, STUN/TURN config, addTransceiver, addTrack, removeTrack, getStats, restartIce, setConfiguration), RTCDataChannel (string + binary send/receive, bufferedAmount, binaryType), RTCRtpSender (track, getParameters/setParameters, replaceTrack, getCapabilities, getStats delegation), RTCRtpReceiver (track with muted→unmuted via ReceiverBridge, jitterBufferTarget, getStats delegation), RTCRtpTransceiver (mid, direction, stop, setCodecPreferences), MediaStream, MediaStreamTrack (GStreamer source integration, enabled→valve), getUserMedia (pipewiresrc/pulsesrc/v4l2src fallback), MediaDevices, **RTCDTMFSender** (spec-compliant tone/duration/gap, `tonechange` event), **RTCCertificate** (generateCertificate, W3C expiry), **RTCDtlsTransport / RTCIceTransport / RTCSctpTransport** (thin proxies), **RTCStatsReport** (GstStructure → W3C camelCase conversion via `gst-stats-parser.ts`). Outgoing pipeline: source→valve→convert→encode(opus/vp8)→payloader→capsfilter→webrtcbin. End-to-end bidirectional audio verified. Registers via `@gjsify/webrtc/register` (granular subpaths) — `--globals auto` picks them up. Requires GStreamer ≥ 1.20 with gst-plugins-bad + libnice-gstreamer. |
| **webrtc-native** | Gst 1.0, GstWebRTC 1.0, GstSDP 1.0 | — | Vala/GObject library consumed by `@gjsify/webrtc`. Exposes three main-thread signal bridges: `WebrtcbinBridge` (wraps webrtcbin's `on-negotiation-needed` / `on-ice-candidate` / `on-data-channel` + `notify::*-state`), `DataChannelBridge` (wraps GstWebRTCDataChannel's `on-open` / `on-close` / `on-error` / `on-message-string` / `on-message-data` / `on-buffered-amount-low` + `notify::ready-state`), `PromiseBridge` (wraps `Gst.Promise.new_with_change_func`). Each bridge connects on the C side (never invokes JS on the streaming thread) and re-emits via `GLib.Idle.add()` on the main context. Ships as prebuilt `.so` + `.typelib` in `prebuilds/linux-{x64,arm64,ppc64,s390x,riscv64}/`; CI (`.github/workflows/prebuilds.yml`) rebuilds on Vala source changes (native runners for x64/arm64; QEMU via `uraimo/run-on-arch-action` for ppc64/s390x/riscv64). |
| **webstorage** | — | 41 | Storage, localStorage, sessionStorage (W3C Web Storage) |
| **webassembly** | — | 15 | WebAssembly Promise-API polyfill — `compile`, `compileStreaming`, `instantiate`, `instantiateStreaming`, `validate` wrap SpiderMonkey's working synchronous `new WebAssembly.Module(buffer)` / `new WebAssembly.Instance(module, imports)` constructors. Granular `/register/promise` subpath. Auto-injected by `--globals auto` whenever the bundle references `WebAssembly.<method>` (via new `METHOD_MARKERS` in `detect-free-globals.ts`). |

### WebRTC Status

**Implemented (Phase 1 + 1.5 — Data Channel end-to-end):**
- RTCPeerConnection: constructor, createOffer, createAnswer, setLocalDescription, setRemoteDescription, addIceCandidate, close, createDataChannel, getConfiguration
- State getters: signalingState, connectionState, iceConnectionState, iceGatheringState, localDescription, remoteDescription, currentLocal/RemoteDescription, pendingLocal/RemoteDescription, canTrickleIceCandidates
- RTCPeerConnection events: negotiationneeded, icecandidate, icegatheringstatechange, iceconnectionstatechange, connectionstatechange, signalingstatechange, datachannel
- RTCDataChannel: send (string / ArrayBuffer / ArrayBufferView / Blob), close, readyState, bufferedAmount, bufferedAmountLowThreshold, binaryType ('arraybuffer' default, 'blob' lazy via globalThis.Blob), id, label, ordered, protocol, negotiated, maxPacketLifeTime, maxRetransmits
- RTCDataChannel events: open, close, message, error, bufferedamountlow, closing
- RTCSessionDescription (with Gst↔JS round-trip via GstSDP), RTCIceCandidate (W3C fields + candidate-line parser), RTCError (extends DOMException), RTCErrorEvent, RTCPeerConnectionIceEvent, RTCDataChannelEvent

**Implemented (Phase 2 — Media API Surface):**
- RTCPeerConnection.addTransceiver(kind, init) — creates real GstWebRTC transceivers, returns RTCRtpTransceiver
- RTCPeerConnection.getSenders / getReceivers / getTransceivers — return live lists
- RTCPeerConnection.removeTrack(sender) — validates sender, resets track
- RTCPeerConnection `track` event — fires on pad-added with RTCTrackEvent (receiver, track, streams, transceiver)
- RTCRtpTransceiver: mid, direction (read/write, mapped to GstWebRTC enum), currentDirection, stop(), setCodecPreferences(codecs)
- RTCRtpSender: track, dtmf, transport, getParameters/setParameters, replaceTrack (stub), getCapabilities(kind)
- RTCRtpReceiver: track (stub MediaStreamTrack, muted), jitterBufferTarget (0–4000ms range), getParameters, getCapabilities(kind)
- MediaStream: id, active, getTracks/getAudioTracks/getVideoTracks, getTrackById, addTrack/removeTrack, clone, addtrack/removetrack events
- MediaStreamTrack: id, kind, label, enabled, muted, readyState, contentHint, clone, stop
- MediaStreamTrackEvent, RTCTrackEvent
- Globals via `@gjsify/webrtc/register/media`: MediaStream, MediaStreamTrack, RTCTrackEvent

**Implemented (Phase 2.5 — Incoming Media Pipeline):**
- ReceiverBridge (Vala): manages muted source → decodebin → tee switching entirely in C to handle decodebin's streaming-thread `pad-added` signal
- RTCRtpReceiver._connectToPad wires webrtcbin output → ReceiverBridge → media-flowing signal → track unmute
- Track transitions from muted to unmuted when decoded media replaces the muted source
- Pipeline cleanup on RTCPeerConnection.close() disposes receiver bridges

**Implemented (Phase 3 — Outgoing Media + getUserMedia):**
- RTCPeerConnection.addTrack(track, ...streams) — creates transceiver, wires outgoing pipeline via request_pad_simple
- getUserMedia({ audio, video }) — wraps GStreamer sources (pipewiresrc → pulsesrc → autoaudiosrc → audiotestsrc fallback; pipewiresrc → v4l2src → autovideosrc → videotestsrc fallback)
- MediaDevices class with getUserMedia, enumerateDevices (stub), getSupportedConstraints
- navigator.mediaDevices registration via `@gjsify/webrtc/register/media-devices`
- Outgoing pipeline: source → valve → audioconvert/videoconvert → encoder (opusenc/vp8enc) → payloader (rtpopuspay/rtpvp8pay) → capsfilter → webrtcbin sink pad
- MediaStreamTrack GStreamer integration: _gstSource, _gstPipeline, enabled→valve.drop, stop()→NULL+dispose
- RTCRtpSender._wirePipeline builds explicit encoder chains (no Vala bridge needed — all main-thread)
- RTCRtpSender.replaceTrack with atomic source swap (unlink old, link new, sync state)
- Capsfilter with RTP caps ensures createOffer generates m= lines immediately
- End-to-end: pcA.addTrack(getUserMedia audio) → pcB receives track event, track unmutes
- Single-PC-per-track limitation (multi-PC fan-out via tee deferred to future)

**Implemented (Phase 4 — Stats & advanced):**
- RTCPeerConnection.getStats() — emits `get-stats` signal on webrtcbin, parses `GstStructure` → `RTCStatsReport` (Map<string, RTCStats>) via `gst-stats-parser.ts` (snake_case → camelCase conversion). `getStats(track)` validates selector against live senders/receivers and rejects with `InvalidAccessError` for unknown tracks. `sender.getStats()` / `receiver.getStats()` delegate via a stats callback wired in `addTrack`/`addTransceiver`.
- RTCPeerConnection.restartIce() — sets ICE restart flag, triggers `negotiationneeded` if negotiation is underway
- RTCPeerConnection.setConfiguration() — validates and applies ICE server updates; rejects immutable fields (`bundlePolicy`, `rtcpMuxPolicy`) with `InvalidModificationError`
- RTCDTMFSender — full spec-compliant tone insertion: DTMF char validation (0-9 A-D # * ,), `duration` clamping (40–6000ms), `interToneGap` (≥30ms), `toneBuffer` reader, `tonechange` event dispatch, `commaDelay` (2 s), `insertDTMF()` overwrites pending queue. Tested against `refs/wpt/webrtc/RTCDTMFSender-insertDTMF.https.html`
- RTCCertificate — `generateCertificate(algorithm)` validates ECDSA/RSASSA-PKCS1-v1_5 params, returns certificate with 30-day expiry. `getFingerprints()`, `expires` getter. W3C API surface matches spec; actual DTLS cert is GStreamer-internal.
- RTCDtlsTransport, RTCIceTransport, RTCSctpTransport — thin W3C proxy classes (state, iceTransport getter, maxMessageSize, maxChannels). Exposed from `@gjsify/webrtc` index.

**Still deferred (post-Phase 4):**
- `icecandidateerror` event — stub (getter returns null, setter no-op); requires mapping webrtcbin ICE failure signals
- `peerIdentity` / `getIdentityAssertion` — stub (rejects with TypeError); identity provider integration not planned
- MediaStreamTrack constraints (`applyConstraints`, `getConstraints`, `getCapabilities` per-device)
- `enumerateDevices` with GStreamer Device Monitor

**Notes on spec behaviour (verified against WPT):**
- RTCDataChannel.binaryType defaults to `'arraybuffer'` — this IS the W3C spec default (§6.2: *"The initial value is 'arraybuffer'"*), distinct from WebSocket which defaults to `'blob'`. Invalid assignments are silently ignored per WPT [RTCDataChannel-binaryType.window.js](refs/wpt/webrtc/RTCDataChannel-binaryType.window.js) (matches Firefox / Chrome / Safari).
- Setting `binaryType` to `'blob'` requires `globalThis.Blob` (provide via `@gjsify/buffer/register`); otherwise the setter throws `NotSupportedError`.
- `setLocalDescription()` without a description argument (implicit createOffer/createAnswer, W3C § 4.4.2) IS implemented, incl. `{ type: 'rollback' }` (webrtcbin handles ROLLBACK natively; verified on GStreamer 1.28).
- Multi-PC-per-track fan-out via tee multiplexer IS implemented (first `addTrack` moves the source, a second concurrent consumer inserts the tee).

**Upstream GStreamer defect — the ONE red test:**
- `wpt.spec.ts` "send/receive empty string" is red and KEPT red on purpose: GStreamer's `GstWebRTCDataChannel` cannot transmit an empty message. Measured on GStreamer 1.28.5: both `send_string("")` and `send_string(NULL)` (the RFC 8831 § 6.6 `STRING_EMPTY`-PPID path) move the channel to CLOSING and every message sent afterwards is silently lost, while markers sent before arrive. Root cause upstream: `webrtcdatachannel.c` builds a ZERO-length buffer for the empty-PPID path, but SCTP cannot carry empty user messages — RFC 8831 § 6.6 requires ONE zero byte with the `WebRTC String Empty`/`Binary Empty` PPID. Not reachable from JS or the Vala bridge (the channel's appsrc/PPID internals are private to webrtcbin). Unblocks with an upstream GStreamer fix; the test asserts the W3C-correct behaviour and gates until then.

**How the GJS streaming-thread issue is solved (Phase 1.5):**

Webrtcbin emits `on-negotiation-needed`, `on-ice-candidate`, `on-data-channel` (and its `Gst.Promise` change_func callbacks) from GStreamer's internal streaming thread. GJS/SpiderMonkey blocks any JS callback invoked from a non-main thread (critical log: *"Attempting to call back into JSAPI on a different thread. … it has been blocked."*) to prevent VM corruption. An in-JS `GLib.idle_add` workaround doesn't help because the callback body itself never runs.

**`@gjsify/webrtc-native`** solves this on the C side: three Vala GObject classes (`WebrtcbinBridge`, `DataChannelBridge`, `PromiseBridge`) connect to the underlying GStreamer signals and `Gst.Promise` change_func, capture their args, then use `GLib.Idle.add()` to re-emit mirror signals on the main GLib context. JS consumers (`@gjsify/webrtc`) connect to those mirror signals and always run on the main thread.

Two subtleties in the bridge design:
1. `WebrtcbinBridge.on_data_channel_cb` wraps the incoming channel in a `DataChannelBridge` *on the streaming thread* before the idle hop — so the bridge's own signal handlers are connected before any `on-message-*` callbacks can fire on the same thread. Without this eager wrap, the first few messages from the remote peer would race the JS-side setup and get dropped.
2. The `GstWebRTCDataChannelState` C enum is **1-based** (`CONNECTING=1 … CLOSED=4`) but the auto-generated TypeScript declaration omits the initialiser and infers 0-based values. `RTCDataChannel` maps against the real 1-based runtime values.

Tests: **307 executed across 4 spec files** (`webrtc.spec.ts` 89, `wpt.spec.ts` 96, `wpt-media.spec.ts` 109, `register.spec.ts` 10 declared `it()`s, plus loop-generated cases), including the full loopback (two local peers, offer/answer, ICE trickle, data-channel open/send/receive/echo). 306 green + 1 red (the upstream GStreamer empty-message defect above); since the `run()` entry fix the suite REPORTS and exits non-zero on failure.

**System prerequisites:**
- GStreamer ≥ 1.20 with **gst-plugins-bad** (for webrtcbin) AND **libnice-gstreamer** (for ICE transport — webrtcbin's state-change to PLAYING fails without it)
- Fedora:   `dnf install gstreamer1-plugins-bad-free gstreamer1-plugins-bad-free-extras libnice-gstreamer1`
- Ubuntu/Debian: `apt install gstreamer1.0-plugins-bad gstreamer1.0-nice`
- Verify:   `gst-inspect-1.0 webrtcbin && gst-inspect-1.0 nicesrc`

Tests that exercise `webrtcbin` (construction, close, loopback, getStats) auto-skip with a clear message if the nice plugin is missing; the remaining 18 tests (RTCSessionDescription, RTCIceCandidate parsing, register-subpath wiring) cover the platform-agnostic code paths.

Re-exports the `@gjsify/stories` contract. No `/register`. Specs on GJS + Node + browser. Step 1 of the renderer-unification refactor; the 3 renderers refactor onto it next (replacing the NS package's lockstep mock-spec) |

## NativeScript Bridge Packages (`packages/nativescript-bridge/`)

Native mobile API wrappers + design-identity widgets for the NativeScript (Android/iOS) runtime axis. `runtimes.nativescript: 'native'`, all other slots `'none'`. Spec tests run on GJS + Node off-device via the `assertNativeScript()` guard.

| **native-platform** | 7 | `isAndroid`/`isIOS`/`isNativeScript`, `assertNativeScript()`, `platformInfo()` (OS version, SDK level, device model, manufacturer) via `android.os.Build` + `UIDevice`. |
| **adwaita-nativescript** | 124 | **Design-identity axis 4** — **42 native Adwaita widgets** for NativeScript (real `StackLayout`/`GridLayout`/`ScrollView`/`WrapLayout`/`ActivityIndicator`/`SegmentedBar`/`Switch`/`TextField`/`Button`/`Label` + native `action()`/`confirm()` dialogs, NOT a webview), parallel to `@gjsify/adwaita-web` — the FULL Libadwaita storybook set. **Rows (faithful):** `AdwPreferencesPage` (`ScrollView`+`StackLayout` wrapper, `addGroup`), `AdwPreferencesGroup` (title header + `.boxed-list`), `AdwActionRow` (`GridLayout` cols `auto, *, auto`, leading PREFIX slot + title+subtitle stack + suffix slot via `setPrefix`/`setSuffix`; used by the storybook header for go-previous + sidebar-toggle), `AdwMenuButton` (extends `AdwImageButton`; tap opens a native `action()` menu built from `menuItems` (`{id?,label}`) + `menuTitle`, emits `menuItemActivated` (`id`/`label`/`index`, id falls back to label) — the Adwaita primary/app-menu `open-menu-symbolic` button, popover approximated by the OS sheet), `AdwIcon` (a non-interactive `Image` rendering a decorative Adwaita symbolic icon via `renderSymbolicIcon` — for a symbolic INSIDE another widget, e.g. the `AdwComboRow`/`AdwExpanderRow` chevrons, the `AdwActionRow`/`AdwButtonRow`/`AdwButtonContent`/`AdwSplitButton` icons, the `AdwStatusPage` empty-state icon; `add(content,name,title?,icon?)`/`addTitled`, first page auto-visible, `visibleChildName`/`visibleChildIndex`/`visibleChild`/`pages`, bounds-guarded, `notify::visible-child`), `AdwViewSwitcherBar` (`Adw.ViewSwitcherBar` narrow policy — a full-width BOTTOM bar of homogeneous icon-OVER-label buttons bound to a stack via `stack`/`setStack`, two-way synced to its `visible-child`, the active label accent-coloured via CSS, a `revealed` flag an adaptive layout flips so a wide header-bar `AdwViewSwitcher` takes over; minor gap: the active ICON stays theme-fg, not accent, since a rasterised bitmap can't recolour per-state without re-pinning off the scheme) — together they are the phone shell `AdwToolbarView` content=`AdwViewStack` + bottomBar=`AdwViewSwitcherBar`. **Navigation (approximated):** `AdwNavigationView` (manual push/pop `View` stack via `visibility`, `add`/`push`/`pop`/`visiblePage`, `notify::visible-page`; COMPROMISE: no slide/back-swipe gesture — an NS `Frame` would give those but imposes Page-routing + its own chrome), `AdwNavigationSplitView`/`AdwOverlaySplitView` (shared `AdwSplitViewBase`, `setSidebar`/`setContent`/`collapsed`/`showSidebar`/`sidebarPosition`, `notify::show-sidebar`; `collapsed` is a flag the consumer toggles — NS has no responsive container — but `AdwBreakpoint` (below) now drives it off the live window width, so a wide tablet/desktop expands to side-by-side panes and a phone collapses, exactly like `Adw.Breakpoint`; `_syncColumns()` orders the two columns per `sidebarPosition` so a trailing (`'end'`) sidebar — the storybook controls — sits FLUSH to the right edge (fixed `auto` column) instead of floating in the expanding one; the overlay honours `show-sidebar` when EXPANDED too (hiding it reflows content full-width, matching `Adw.OverlaySplitView`); the collapsed overlay anchors `sidebarPosition='end'` right and SLIDES the sidebar in/out + fades a tap-to-dismiss SCRIM, while the navigation variant SLIDES the master↔detail swap — both via the native `View.animate()` API (real per-property animation, NOT the CSS subset; instant `visibility` swap off-screen/off-device); remaining overlay COMPROMISE: no blur/shadow, the opaque sidebar paints last in add-order), **`AdwBreakpoint`** (NS port of `Adw.Breakpoint` + `Adw.BreakpointCondition` — the responsive primitive a ported GNOME app needs: `parseBreakpointCondition('max-width: 720sp')` parses the Libadwaita grammar — `min/max-width/height` leaves with px/sp/pt read as DIPs, `and`/`or` (`or` looser) + parens; `evaluateBreakpointCondition`; `AdwBreakpoint(condition, {onApply, onUnapply})` fires only on a state transition; `addBreakpoints(view, [...])` drives them off the view's post-layout size via its `layoutChanged` event — NS has no window-size signal — with a `loaded` seed, returns a dispose fn; type-only `@nativescript/core` import so it unit-tests off-device), `AdwSidebar` (scrollable boxed nav list, `setItems`/`selected`, `notify::selected`), `AdwBottomSheet` (bottom-anchored `StackLayout` panel + drag handle, `setContent`/`setSheet`/`open`/`close`, `notify::open`; COMPROMISE: instant `visibility` show/hide, no slide-up animation or dimming scrim). **Feedback/dialogs:** `AdwToast`+`AdwToastOverlay` (bottom snackbar strip, `showToast(title,{timeout,buttonLabel})`/`addToast(toast)`, one-at-a-time queued auto-dismiss + optional action button — the queue + auto-dismiss lifecycle + `AdwToast` value object are HEADLESS (`@gjsify/adwaita-core` `AdwToastQueue`, ADR 0004); NS keeps the `GridLayout` render + the `setTimeout`-backed `ToastScheduler` that drives it; COMPROMISE: instant appear/disappear, no slide), `AdwAlertDialog` (extends `Observable` — NOT a `View`; `heading`/`body`/`addResponse`/`present()→Promise<id>`, `notify::response`; APPROXIMATED via native `confirm()` for ≤3 responses mapped to OK/neutral/cancel else an `action()` sheet — honest on-platform OS chrome, NOT an in-app modal — the response registry + ordering + resolve-to-chosen-id contract are HEADLESS (`@gjsify/adwaita-core` `AdwAlertResponses`, ADR 0004), NS keeps only the native `confirm()`/`action()` mapping), `AdwAboutDialog`/`AdwPreferencesDialog` (in-page modal overlay card with dimmed scrim + a rounded-square `AdwImageButton` window-close button, `present()`/`close()`/`open`; AboutDialog: `applicationName`/`applicationIcon`-glyph/`version`/`developerName`/`comments`/`website`/`copyright`; PreferencesDialog hosts `AdwPreferencesPage`s via `add()`; COMPROMISE: no backdrop-blur/drop-shadow/slide-in animation — they are in-page overlays the consumer adds to their root layout, not separate OS windows). All Material buttons set `androidElevation:0` (Adwaita = flat, no Material shadow/ripple). Boxed-list rows: tight 10×16 padding + a 1px top-border hairline separator drawn only BETWEEN rows (light + dark) — `AdwPreferencesGroup._refreshRowEdges()` flags the FIRST row `.adw-row-flush-top` so the separator is not stroked above it (NS does not clip the first row's top border under the card's rounded corner, where it would read as a stray line); rows are TRANSPARENT (the `.boxed-list` card paints the rounded fill — NS does NOT clip children to a parent's border-radius, so an opaque row would overflow the rounded corners and read as a sharp card). `registerAdwaitaElements()` wires the global `registerElement` for XML use — only for the `View`-extending widgets (the `Observable`-based `AdwAlertDialog` + the value-object `AdwToast` are excluded). Two shared abstract bases (`AdwViewSwitcherBase`, `AdwSplitViewBase`) are exported but not registered. Static `theme/adwaita.css` (NS CSS subset — color/bg/font/padding/margin/size/radius/opacity/border only, light + `.ns-dark` for EVERY widget). **CSS-subset compromises (apply to ALL approximated widgets):** no `transform`/animation/gradient/shadow/blur/z-index/focus-ring *in CSS* → most reveals are instant `visibility` toggles (no animated slide/cross-fade) — EXCEPT the split-views, which slide + scrim-fade via the native `View.animate()` API (a real per-property animation outside the CSS subset; off-screen/off-device they fall back to the instant swap), overlays still rely on add-order painting + opaque fills (no real layering blur), the avatar derives its colour from the name exactly like `Adw.Avatar` (`g_str_hash(text) % 14` over the 14-colour libadwaita palette), filled flat with the 50/50 blend of the palette gradient (NS has no gradient), buttons AND activatable boxed-list rows (`AdwButtonRow`/`AdwComboRow`) + sidebar nav rows darken on press via the `:highlighted` pseudo-class (Adwaita's `:active` shade — `$view_active_color` = currentColor 8% for rows / `--button-active-color` for buttons, light + dark; opaque rows pre-blend the 8% over their white/dark base → `#ebebeb`/`#44444a`). NS only auto-applies `:highlighted` to `Button`; the rows wire it by hand via `attachRowPressFeedback()` (a shared `touch`-gesture listener — `highlighted` on `down`, cleared on `up`/`cancel`; the platform's ScrollView touch-slop → `ACTION_CANCEL` clears a press-then-scroll). No hover state (touch has none) and no focus ring (CSS subset). Icons are REAL Adwaita symbolic icons rendered natively (NS `Image` has no SVG decoder, so `renderSymbolicIcon()` (`icons.android.ts`) parses each `<path>` of the symbolic SVG with `androidx.core.graphics.PathParser`, scales the 16-grid path to device pixels, and fills it per-path honouring `fill-opacity` onto a `Bitmap` → `ImageSource`), surfaced as `AdwImageButton` (tappable rounded-square icon button), `AdwIcon` (decorative icon inside a widget) and `AdwButtonContent` (icon+label). The widget set uses them for the action-row prefix/suffix, the spin-row ± stepper, the expander/combo/split-button chevrons, the button-row/button-content/split-button icons, the status-page empty-state icon and the dialog close — replacing the earlier emoji-glyph stand-ins. Because the symbolic is rasterised to a pre-coloured bitmap, the caller may set an explicit `iconColor` context colour (white on a suggested button, destructive-red on a destructive row); OTHERWISE the icon's default fill FOLLOWS the active color scheme — `AdwIcon` / `AdwImageButton` re-render the bitmap in the light/dark fg via the pure `color-scheme` module (`setAdwaitaColorScheme` / `adwaitaColorScheme` / `themeIconColor` / `onAdwaitaColorSchemeChanged`; icons subscribe while on screen + unsubscribe on unload, so the registry never leaks), so a dark-theme app shows light icons and vice-versa. An explicit `iconColor` PINS the colour through both schemes (the accent/destructive/white/dim-grey context colours stay put). The remaining glyph stand-ins are the carousel dots (`●`) and the bottom-sheet drag handle (`━`), where a glyph is the right primitive. Adwaita Sans via `@gjsify/adwaita-fonts` TTFs. `@nativescript/core` = optional peer (ambient `src/ns-core.d.ts` now also adds `WrapLayout`/`AbsoluteLayout`/`ActivityIndicator`/`Image`/`SegmentedBar`/`SegmentedBarItem`/`confirm()`/`ScrollView.scrollTo*`/`LayoutBase.insertChild`/`GridLayout.removeColumns()`/`Label.textAlignment` + the animation surface `View.animate()`/`translateX`/`translateY`/`isLoaded`/`getActualSize()` + the press surface `View.addPseudoClass()`/`deletePseudoClass()` + the `'touch'` gesture's `TouchGestureEventData` + the image surface `ImageSource`/`Image.imageSource`/`Screen.mainScreen.scale` + `Slider` to keep `gjsify tsc` green off-device). 124 specs — the breakpoint parser/evaluator/state-machine + color-scheme observable + toast queue (`AdwToast`/`AdwToastQueue`) + alert-dialog response model (`AdwAlertResponses`) + the row interaction state machines (`ExpanderState`/`ComboState`/`SpinState`/`ToggleGroupState`; the expander/combo/spin/toggle-group widgets now compose + re-export them, keeping only their `GridLayout`/`Label`/`AdwImageButton`/`AdwIcon` render + `notify::*` wiring) matrices moved to `@gjsify/adwaita-core` (ADR 0004; re-exported here, no consumer break, pinned by re-export smoke specs) — incl. `addBreakpoints` wire+dispose (NS view binding) + AdwViewStack/AdwViewSwitcherBar (decoupled selection + two-way bind) + AdwMenuButton (action() activation). |
| **devtools-nativescript** | 6 | **In-app devtools agent** — NS analogue of `@gjsify/devtools` (GTK). `installDevtools({ application, frame })` (env-gated `GJSIFY_DEVTOOLS`) attaches `globalThis.__adwDevtools.dispatch(reqJson)`, routing through the SAME `@gjsify/devtools-protocol` `MethodRegistry` the GTK adapter uses. `GetStatus`/`ListToplevels`/`DumpTree`/`GetProperty` walk the NS view tree via `view.eachChildView()` (stable `toplevel:0/child:N` paths → shared `NodeInfo`); `Screenshot` → base64 PNG (`screenshot.android.ts` = `android.graphics.Bitmap`+`Canvas`+`view.android.draw`; `screenshot.ios.ts` = `UIGraphicsImageRenderer`, **UNTESTED**; `platformResolvePlugin` picks the variant, base `screenshot.ts` throws `Platform not supported`); `ActivateAction` = best-effort `view.notify({eventName:'tap'})`. Reached by the host `@gjsify/devtools-mcp` `nativescriptProfile` over the V8 CDP inspector. `@nativescript/core` optional peer (view tree reached structurally — no value import). 6 specs: install guard + registry round-trip on a mock view tree (GJS + Node). |
| **storybook-nativescript** | 10 | **NativeScript renderer for the `@gjsify/stories` contract** — the NS counterpart of `@gjsify/storybook` (GTK) + `@gjsify/adwaita-storybook` (browser), rendering the SAME stories (shared renderer-agnostic `*.meta.ts`) in a native Adwaita component browser built from REAL `@gjsify/adwaita-nativescript` widgets (NOT a webview) so the three targets screenshot-compare 1:1. `StoryView` (extends NS `StackLayout`) = the NS twin of browser `StoryElement` / GTK `StoryWidget` with the IDENTICAL authoring surface (`(meta, story='Default', customRoot?)` ctor; `meta`/`story`/`args` getters; `args` setter→`updateArgs`+notify; `onArgsChanged`/`setArg`; overridable `initialize`/`updateArgs`/`teardown`; `addContent`) → a `*.ns.ts` story is a near-1:1 port of its `*.web.ts` twin. Default chrome = an `AdwClamp` wrapping a transparent group with the title + description as BARE text (NOT an `AdwPreferencesGroup` card — only a story's OWN widgets carry a boxed-list card, matching the GTK/browser twins) above a centered dashed-frame `.story-stage` (1px dashed `rgba(0,0,0,.28)`, 18px padding — matching the GTK `.story-stage`). `StoryRegistry` (one-per-run, no singleton; category grouping + decorators). `createControlRow` maps each `StoryControl` kind to a live two-way-bound Adwaita row — TEXT→`AdwEntryRow`, BOOLEAN→`AdwSwitchRow`, NUMBER→`AdwSpinRow`, RANGE→`AdwSliderRow` (real slider, matching the GTK/browser RANGE card), SELECT→`AdwComboRow`, COLOR→`AdwEntryRow` hex-fallback (NS has no native color/slider row). `StorybookNativeApp`/`runStorybook(options)` lay out an ADAPTIVE `AdwNavigationSplitView` driven by an `AdwBreakpoint` (`max-width: 720sp`, the SAME condition as the GTK storybook's `Adw.Breakpoint`). NARROW (<720sp, phone): collapsed master⇄detail — tapping a story in the master SIDEBAR navigates to the DETAIL pane, a back button returns; the controls are a tap-to-reveal right overlay. WIDE (>=720sp, tablet/desktop): sidebar + preview + controls sit SIDE BY SIDE (three panes) — tapping a story swaps the preview without hiding the sidebar, the back button is hidden, and the `☰` toggle hides/shows the controls pane — exactly the native GTK storybook un-collapsed. The mode is seeded from the screen width at construction (no phone-layout flash on a wide screen) and kept exact by the breakpoint on the root's live width (verified rotating a phone across the threshold flips it live). Each pane is an `AdwToolbarView` carrying its OWN `AdwHeaderBar` → a SINGLE header bar per pane (page `ActionBar` hidden): master header titled, detail header = back + story title + a controls `☰` toggle. Detail content is an `AdwOverlaySplitView` (`sidebarPosition='end'`) whose content is the preview and whose RIGHT side is the live CONTROLS panel (a tap-to-reveal overlay when collapsed, a permanent right pane when expanded) — the GTK preview NavigationPage + its OverlaySplitView controls sidebar. Exposes the same MCP/devtools control surface as the browser app (`listStories`/`openStoryByTitle`/`getCurrentStory`/`setActiveArg`/`activeStory`). Re-exports the `@gjsify/stories` contract (one import per story). Static `theme/storybook.css` (NS CSS subset, `.sb-*`+`.story-stage` chrome, light + `.ns-dark`) tuned to match the native GTK sidebar: selected story rows use the Adwaita GREY `$selected_color` (currentColor 10%) not the accent, with press-darken wired via `attachRowPressFeedback`; category separators are Adwaita `.heading` body-size bold+dim (not a shrunk caption); the controls panel uses `secondary_sidebar_bg_color` (`#f3f3f5`). The showcase's story list is ordered to match the native sidebar exactly (alphabetical by `<category>/<story>` path, like the GTK glob). FOLLOWS the OS color scheme like a real Adwaita app: pass the NS `Application` as `runStorybook({ application })` (structural `NsAppearanceSource`, no `@nativescript/core` value import) and the storybook seeds the icon scheme from `Application.systemAppearance()` at mount — NS owns the `ns-dark` CSS class on the root (backgrounds/text), this drives the half CSS can't reach (the pre-coloured symbolic ICON bitmaps recolour to the light/dark fg). Seed-only at mount: a clean launch is the dependable path because NS 9.1-alpha does not reliably re-apply the `.ns-dark` overrides at runtime, so a live OS theme switch is picked up on the next launch. `@nativescript/core` optional peer (ambient `src/ns-core.d.ts` superset of the adwaita slice). 10 specs: `argsFromControls` defaults + category grouping + control-row arg binding (mock `StoryView`) + `isNsStoryModule` (GJS + Node). |

## Browser UI Packages (`packages/web/adwaita-web/`)

| Package | Tests | APIs |
|---------|-------|------|
| **adwaita-web** | — | AdwWindow, AdwHeaderBar, AdwPreferencesGroup, AdwSwitchRow, AdwComboRow, AdwSpinRow, AdwToastOverlay, AdwOverlaySplitView, AdwCard. **Aligned data grid:** **AdwDataGrid** (`<adw-data-grid>`) — a slim, Adwaita-styled ALIGNED numeric grid for genuinely tabular financial data (a BWA / P&L statement, invoice line-items), the web mirror of the native `Gtk.Grid` the Buchhaltung app fills; DELIBERATELY NOT a sortable Gtk.ColumnView (boxed lists stay the default for record lists). `columns` (`{key,label?,align?,width?,flex?,monospace?,numeric?}` — numeric ⇒ right-aligned + tabular figures so columns of numbers line up), `rows` (row objects keyed by column key + optional accounting `variant` `normal`/`section` (bold spanning group header)/`subtotal` (bold + hairline rule above)/`total` (bold + stronger double rule)), pre-formatted STRING cells (locale currency stays in the app; the grid does layout + alignment + emphasis only), `caption`, `interactive` + per-row `interactive` opt-in/out → `row-activated` `{index,row}` for master-detail; NO clickable-sortable headers (sorting is an external data op). CSS-subgrid rows so figures align across real row elements; horizontal scroll contained within its own container; sits inside an `<adw-card>`; light + dark via CSS vars — unblocks the Buchhaltung BWA + invoice-table web rewrite. **Nav/shell (view-switching):** AdwViewSwitcher, AdwInlineViewSwitcher + **AdwViewStack** (standalone named-page content container, `add`/`addTitled`/`visibleChildName`/`visibleChildIndex`/`pages`, first page auto-visible, `notify::visible-child`), **AdwViewSwitcherBar** (narrow BOTTOM bar of icon-over-label buttons bound to an `<adw-view-stack>` via `stack`/`setStack`, two-way synced, accent active label, `revealed` toggle) and **AdwMenuButton** (flat `open-menu` popover app-menu — `menuItems` `{id?,label,icon?}`, `menuTitle`, `menu-item-activated` `{id,label,index}`, outside-click/Escape dismiss + arrow-key nav) — the phone-shell trio (`ViewStack` content + bottom `ViewSwitcherBar` + header `MenuButton`) mirroring the NS `adwaita-nativescript` twins, for the **Learn6502 web rewrite** (ADR 0007 successor). **Dialogs:** AdwAboutDialog, AdwAlertDialog, AdwPreferencesDialog + **AdwDialog** (`<adw-dialog>` — the generic adaptive `Adw.Dialog` base: default-slot content + optional header/title, `present()`/`close()`/`forceClose()`, `can-close` gating (Escape/scrim/close-button → `close-attempt` while locked), `content-width`/`content-height`, `presentation-mode` floating card on wide ↔ bottom sheet on narrow via a CSS media query, scrim + focus-trap + return-focus, `notify::open`/`closed`). **Controls:** AdwButton, AdwEntry, AdwToggleGroup, AdwSplitButton + **AdwDropDown** (`<adw-drop-down>` — the standalone `Gtk.DropDown` mirror, the inline/toolbar twin of `<adw-combo-row>`: a flat button showing the selection + a `pan-down` chevron dropping an Adwaita popover list; `options`/`items` (`{value,label}` or `string[]`, JSON attr or property), `selected`/`selectedValue`/`selectedItem`, optional `enable-search`, arrow/Home/End/Enter/Escape nav + type-ahead, outside-click/Escape dismiss, `notify::selected` + `change` `{index,value,label}`). AdwDialog + AdwDropDown unblock BOTH the Learn6502 web rewrite AND the Buchhaltung accounting app (both previously hand-rolled a modal + a `<select>`). DOM behaviour tests via the browser test axis (`src/*.spec.ts` → `dist/test.browser.mjs`, `tests/browser` Playwright). **Editor (opt-in subpath `@gjsify/adwaita-web/source-view`, NOT in the core bundle):** **AdwSourceView** (`<adw-source-view>`) — the GtkSourceView twin for the Learn6502 web rewrite, a CodeMirror-6-backed editor mapping 1:1 onto the `common-ui` `SourceViewWidget` interface (`code`/`editable`/`readonly`/`selectable`/`lineNumbers`/`lineNumberStart`/`copyable`/`copyButtonIcon`/`copyButtonTooltip`) with a **hex-address gutter mode** (`hexAddresses` — line numbers render as 4-digit hex addresses from `lineNumberStart`, for the monitor/hexdump/disassembled panes), a **6502 assembly `StreamLanguage`** mode (opcodes/registers/`$`·`%`·`#` literals/labels/comments/directives), an **Adwaita CodeMirror theme** mapping editor colours to the adwaita-web CSS variables so it follows light+dark automatically, `code-changed` on user edits (programmatic `.code=` suppressed for two-way binding), a floating copy button with **copy-without-spaces in hex mode**, and LTR-pinning. CodeMirror is opt-in (`@codemirror/{state,view,language,commands}` + `@lezer/highlight`) so it never bloats the core bundle; self-injects its own stylesheet. Custom Elements (light DOM). SCSS source partials in `scss/` (mirroring `refs/adwaita-web/scss/`) compiled to `dist/adwaita-web.css` via the `sass` package. Light/dark theme via CSS variables. Consumers import `@gjsify/adwaita-web` (registers custom elements + Adwaita Sans font) plus `@gjsify/adwaita-web/style.css` (or via SCSS partials at `@gjsify/adwaita-web/scss/*`); the editor via `@gjsify/adwaita-web/source-view`. No GJS deps |
| **adwaita-fonts** | — | Adwaita Sans font files (fontsource-style). CSS @font-face + TTF files. SIL OFL 1.1 |
| **adwaita-icons** | — | Adwaita symbolic icons as importable SVG strings (categories: actions, devices, mimetypes, places, status, ui, …). `toDataUri()` utility. Sourced from `refs/adwaita-icon-theme/`. CC0-1.0 / LGPLv3 |
| **adwaita-core** | 218 | **Headless Adwaita widget behavior (ADR 0004)** — pure TS, NO platform imports (no `gi://`, no `@girs/*`, no `@nativescript/core`, no DOM), all-runtime `polyfill`, `tier: 2`, no `/register`, pure named exports (the `storybook-core` seam pattern applied to the design-identity axis). Modules moved out of `adwaita-nativescript`: **breakpoint conditions** (`parseBreakpointCondition` — Libadwaita grammar: `min/max-width/height` leaves, `px`/`sp`/`pt` read as DIPs, `and`/`or` (`or` looser) + parens; `evaluateBreakpointCondition`; transition-only `AdwBreakpoint` apply/unapply state machine driven by renderer-fed `{width, height}` samples), the **color-scheme observable** (`setAdwaitaColorScheme`/`adwaitaColorScheme`/`toggleAdwaitaColorScheme`/`themeIconColor`/`isThemeIconColor`/`onAdwaitaColorSchemeChanged` + the `DEFAULT_ICON_COLOR`/`DEFAULT_ICON_COLOR_DARK` fg constants; throw-safe fan-out), the **toast queue** (`AdwToast` value object incl. `hasButton` action-button state + `AdwToastQueue` — `Adw.ToastOverlay`'s one-at-a-time policy + FIFO ordering + the auto-dismiss lifecycle state machine, `add`/`dismiss`/`clear`/`current`/`showing`/`pending`; timing is INJECTED via a `ToastScheduler` seam — core NEVER calls a global `setTimeout` — and render via `onShow`/`onHide` handlers, mirroring how the breakpoint machine takes renderer-fed samples; `DEFAULT_TOAST_TIMEOUT`), the **alert-dialog response model** (`AdwAlertResponses` — the response registry `addResponse(id, label, {appearance, enabled})` with `set/getResponseAppearance`/`set/getResponseEnabled`, default/close-response semantics, the OK/cancel/neutral `orderResponses()` split, the `usesActionSheet` (>3) threshold, and the resolve-to-chosen-id contract `resolveById`/`resolveLabel`; `AdwResponseAppearance` suggested/destructive), and the **row interaction state machines** (`rows.ts` — `ExpanderState` (expanded/collapsed disclosure, idempotent `toggle`/`expand`/`collapse`, notify on change), `ComboState` (options list + two-way `selectedIndex`↔`selectedValue`/`selectedLabel` mapping with empty/out-of-range guards; `AdwComboOption`; a programmatic `setSelectedIndex`/`setSelectedValue`/`setOptions` notifies `interactive:false` while a user `select()` notifies `interactive:true`), `SpinState` (`value`/`min`/`max`/`step` clamped on every mutation, `increment`/`decrement` stepping — interactive — vs `setValue`/`setMin`/`setMax` — programmatic; step falls back to 1), `ToggleGroupState` (label list + guarded selected index, `setLabels` silent reset vs `setSelected` notify)). Each row machine notifies through a per-instance `subscribe(listener)`→unsubscribe seam (no globals/timers); `SpinState`/`ComboState` tag each change `interactive` so a renderer re-emits `notify::*` only on a user-driven change (preserving the NS programmatic-set-is-silent fidelity). `@gjsify/adwaita-nativescript` re-exports the full surface (no consumer break); NS keeps only the platform binding (`addBreakpoints` view wiring, the `AdwToastOverlay` `GridLayout` render + `setTimeout` scheduler, the `AdwAlertDialog` native `confirm()`/`action()` mapping + `notify::response`, and the row widgets' `GridLayout`/`Label`/`AdwImageButton`/`AdwIcon`/press-feedback render + `notify::*` wiring, all driven by the core state objects; `ns-dark`/bitmap re-render stays with its owners). ADR 0004 fully implemented. Specs on GJS + Node |

Long-term goal: complete the `@gjsify/adwaita-web` framework so it can replace the styling layer of `refs/adwaita-web/scss/` while keeping our Web Components abstraction. Currently 9 components ported; ~40 SCSS partials remain in the reference. Planned port order (each adds a custom element + SCSS partial + AGENTS attribution):

| ✅ Done | `<adw-window>`, `<adw-header-bar>`, `<adw-preferences-group>`, `<adw-card>`, `<adw-switch-row>`, `<adw-combo-row>`, `<adw-spin-row>`, `<adw-toast-overlay>`, `<adw-overlay-split-view>` | `_window.scss`, `_headerbar.scss`, `_preferences.scss`, `_card.scss`, `_switch_row.scss`, `_combo_row.scss`, `_spin_button.scss`, `_toast.scss`, (libadwaita C source) |

### WebGL Known Issues

Issues discovered while porting Three.js demos. Non-fatal but should be addressed for full compatibility.

| Issue | Severity | Details | Affected Demos |
|-------|----------|---------|----------------|
| `EXT_color_buffer_float` extension missing | Medium | Three.js requests this extension for `HalfFloatType` render targets. Not implemented in extension registry. Rendering works but with fallback quality. | LDraw, Pixel Post-Processing |
| WebGL1 `setError` calls too strict for WebGL2 | Low | Base class validation (texImage2D, renderbufferStorage, etc.) uses WebGL1 format/type rules. WebGL2 allows more combinations (R8, RG8, RGBA16F, DEPTH_COMPONENT24, etc.). Non-fatal — native GL still executes the calls. | All WebGL2 demos |
| WebGL1 framebuffer color attachment validation too strict | Low | Base `_preCheckFramebufferStatus` only accepts RGBA/UNSIGNED_BYTE or RGBA/FLOAT. WebGL2 override delegates to native driver. WebGL1 with extensions (OES_texture_half_float) still rejects valid formats. | Post-processing with WebGL1 |

### Missing Web APIs

Not yet implemented (but potentially relevant for GJS projects):

| API | Priority | Notes |
|-----|----------|-------|
| ~~**ECDSA sign/verify**~~ | ✓ Done | Implemented in Phase 19 (RFC 6979 + FIPS 186-4) |
| ~~**RSA-PSS / RSA-OAEP**~~ | ✓ Done | Implemented in Phase 19 (RFC 8017, MGF1) |
| **URL/URLSearchParams (global)** | Low | Exists in @gjsify/url, missing as global export |
| **Blob/File (global)** | Low | Partially native in GJS; globals package could re-export |
| **structuredClone** | Low | Natively available in SpiderMonkey 128 |
| **Performance (global)** | Low | @gjsify/perf_hooks exists; Web export missing |

| **vite-plugin-gjsify** | Vite preset (`gjsifyBrowser()`) — Vite-dev/HMR parity with `gjsify build --app browser` (mirrors the browser-target composition, minus css-as-string) | Full |
| **vite-plugin-gettext** | xgettext/msgfmt/po2json pipeline (Vite/Rollup/Rolldown) | Full |
| **resolve-npm** | Central alias registry (60+ mappings) | Full |
| **empty** | Stub module for platform exclusion | Full |
| **lightningcss-native** | Phase D-2 POC. Vala+Rust cdylib bridge to lightningcss for GJS. Two GObject methods: `Engine.transform()` (in-memory CSS string → CSS string) and `Engine.bundle()` (entry CSS file → bundled CSS, resolves `@import` chains via lightningcss's filesystem-backed `FileProvider`). Both accept browserslist queries directly. Backs `cssAsStringPlugin` under `--app gjs`: because `bundle()`'s `FileProvider` cannot resolve bare node_modules specifiers (`@import "@scope/pkg/x.css"`, a JS resolver callback can't cross the GI/Rust boundary), the plugin resolves + inlines every `@import` in JS first (its shared `cssBundleResolver`, npm-package + `exports`-map aware) and routes the flattened CSS through `Engine.transform()` for the GTK4 lowering; falls back to the npm `lightningcss` `bundleAsync` path when `hasNativeLightningcss()` is false. Ships Rust `cdylib` (`libgjsify_lightningcss.so`, ~8.3 MB) + Vala bridge (`libgjsifylightningcss.so`, ~34 KB) + GIR/typelib in `prebuilds/linux-x64/`. The TS facade reuses ONE lazily-created `Engine` across all `transform()`/`bundle()` calls instead of constructing one per call — sound because the engine is stateless at every layer (Vala class with zero fields, stateless C glue, free Rust `extern "C"` fns with no statics and `ParserOptions.warnings: None`), and pinned by the package's own GJS spec (12 tests: A→B→A option-independence for targets/minify/sourceMap/filename, error isolation, bundle↔transform interop, plus a construction counter). POC-only: no source-map input, no css-modules, no analyze-dependencies — those are Phase B work. | POC |
| **lightningcss-wasm** | Phase D-2 POC (WASM track companion to `lightningcss-native`). Vendors `lightningcss-wasm@1.32.0`'s NAPI-on-WASM bundle (15.8 MiB `.wasm` + the napi-wasm runtime + the asyncify async helper). Pure-JS loader instantiates the WASM via SpiderMonkey 140's synchronous `new WebAssembly.{Module,Instance}` constructors; reads bytes via `node:fs` (resolves to `@gjsify/fs` under `--app gjs`); resolves `__getrandom_v03_custom` via `globalThis.crypto.getRandomValues` (`@gjsify/webcrypto` under `--app gjs`). **No WASI shim required** — napi-wasm is a pure-JS NAPI host. Smoke tested under stock `gjs -m`: 0.81 ms for nesting flatten + minify of a 90 B fixture. Decision matrix: **3-5× slower than native** on transforms, ~960× slower cold init — kept in tree as a fallback for unsupported architectures. | POC |
| **rolldown-native** | Phase D-2 POC. Vala+Rust cdylib bridge to rolldown for GJS. Wraps `rolldown::Bundler::generate()` (async, tokio) in a per-call current-thread runtime so JS sees a sync `bundle()` call. JSON-encoded `BundlerOptions` in (camelCase, matches rolldown's serde shape), JSON-encoded BundleOutput out (`{warnings, output: (Chunk\|Asset)[]}`). Ships Rust cdylib (`libgjsify_rolldown.so`, ~13 MB) + Vala bridge + GIR/typelib in `prebuilds/linux-x64/`. Smoke tested under GJS 1.88: 17 ms cold for a 3-file ESM bundle with constant-inlining + tree-shake; 25-byte minified output for a single-export entry; clear UnresolvedEntry diagnostics on missing inputs. **No longer POC — JS plugins (Phase B) shipped + it is the DEFAULT bundler engine under GJS** (Phase D-3.1): `bundleWithPlugins()` drives a `BundlerSession` GObject (`hook_requested`/`context_response` signals + payload stashing) running all 12 rolldown hook positions, so gjsify's full plugin set runs via the native engine; `cli/src/bundler-pick.ts` `shouldUseNative()` picks native under GJS automatically (npm rolldown is a Rust N-API addon that cannot load under GJS), npm under Node, with `GJSIFY_BUNDLER=native|npm` as an explicit override. `runNativeBundle` writes the BundleOutput to disk replicating npm rolldown's `.write()` incl. nested chunk/asset subdirectories (`--library`/code-split — fixed: it previously ENOENT'd on the missing parent of e.g. `_virtual/_rolldown/runtime.js`; regression-covered in `tests/e2e/self-host`). Still no watch/HMR (`--watch` requires npm rolldown under Node) and no incremental builds. **Build constraint**: depends on `refs/rolldown` submodule via cargo path-dep — rolldown 0.1.0 on crates.io has broken transitive deps (rolldown_fs/oxc_resolver_fs trait mismatch). End consumers receive only the prebuilt `.so` + `.typelib` and never see the submodule. **Platform:** the wakeup channel driving the plugin bridge is now three anonymous pipes (`src/rust/src/wakeup.rs`) instead of three `libc::eventfd(2)` descriptors — ONE portable implementation on every platform, so the crate compiles for Apple targets (`cargo check --target aarch64-apple-darwin` green) and `meson.build`'s darwin branch is no longer dead code. Both ends are `O_NONBLOCK` + `FD_CLOEXEC`; the writer drops an `EAGAIN` wake (a full buffer means a wakeup is already pending, and the producer enqueues before waking while the Vala watch drains the pipe before re-draining the queue — that ordering is what makes the drop safe), and the watch now drains to `G_IO_STATUS_AGAIN` instead of reading a single 8-byte eventfd-shaped chunk, which is what preserves eventfd's coalescing. Still Linux-only in `gjsify.platforms` until the `prebuilds.yml` darwin leg builds + load-tests the `.dylib`/`.typelib`. | Default engine under GJS |
| **oxfmt-native** | Vala+Rust cdylib GI bridge to oxc's formatter (`@gjsify/oxfmt-native`) — the Node-free engine behind `gjsify format`/`gjsify fix` under GJS (the npm `oxfmt` is a Rust napi binary GJS cannot load). The Rust shim links the **pure-Rust oxfmt CLI core** (`oxfmt::cli`, `default-features = false` → napi OFF) via Cargo path-deps into `refs/oxc`, pinned to `oxfmt_v0.54.0` = the locked npm `oxfmt@0.54.0` (keep tag ↔ devDep in lockstep when bumping). Surface: `Formatter.run(args) → exit code` — the FULL oxfmt CLI in-process (`.oxfmtrc(.json)`/`.editorconfig` resolution, ignore handling, parallel rayon walking, `--write`/`--check`/`--list-different`, stdout/stderr reporting identical to the npm launcher) + `Formatter.format(filename, bytes)` — single-shot in-memory format with default options. NOT covered (napi-only upstream by design): the embedded CSS/HTML/Vue/Markdown Prettier `ExternalFormatter`, `.oxfmtrc.ts` JS configs, `--init`/`--migrate` (the CLI's own templates handle init), LSP + stdin mode. `cli/src/utils/oxc-resolve.ts` `shouldUseNativeOxfmt()` mirrors `bundler-pick.ts`: native under GJS when loadable, npm launcher on Node, `GJSIFY_OXFMT=native|npm` override. Ships Rust cdylib (`libgjsify_oxfmt.so`, ~5.4 MB) + Vala bridge (`libgjsifyoxfmt.so`) + GIR/typelib in `prebuilds/linux-x64/` (arm64 via `prebuilds.yml` — see Open TODOs); optional peer dep of `@gjsify/cli`. 5 Rust unit tests (`cargo test`: single-shot TS/TSX + in-process run write/help/parse-error). | Default format engine under GJS |

**GJS Infrastructure:**

| Package | Function | Status |
|---------|----------|--------|
| **@gjsify/unit** | Test framework (describe/it/expect) + vitest-compat surface (`vi.fn`/`stubGlobal`/`stubEnv`, `toMatchObject`/`toBeNaN`/`toHaveBeenCalled*`, `expect().rejects.toThrow`/`.resolves.toResolve`) | Full |
| **@gjsify/utils** | Gio wrappers, process info, encoding, ensureMainLoop | Full |
| **@gjsify/runtime** | Platform-independent runtime detection (isGJS, isNode, runtimeName) | Full |
| **@gjsify/types** | GIR TypeScript bindings | Manual |

| **`@gjsify/http-soup-bridge` (Vala)** | http (libsoup server bridge — GC-safe SoupServerMessage lifetime, SSE/long-poll peer-close detection via `g_socket_create_source`) |
| **`@gjsify/http2-native` (Vala + C shim)** | http2 (libnghttp2 HPACK encoder + raw frame builder for PUSH_PROMISE / DATA / HEADERS, server-side even-id allocator, h2c connection-preface detection — buffers C-side via GLib.Bytes) |
| **`@gjsify/terminal-native` (Vala)** | tty, process (optional terminal syscalls — Posix.isatty, ioctl TIOCGWINSZ, termios raw mode, SIGWINCH; GLib/env fallback when not installed) |
| **`@gjsify/lightningcss-native` (Vala + Rust cdylib)** | Phase D-2 POC bridge to the `lightningcss` crate. Rust shim (`src/rust/`, `lightningcss = "1"` from crates.io) exposes a one-shot extern `gjsify_lightningcss_transform()`; C glue (`src/vala/gjsify-lightningcss-glue.c`) translates the malloc'd Rust struct into `GBytes` + `GError`; Vala wraps that as `GjsifyLightningcss.Engine.transform()`. Used today only by future Phase B `cssAsStringPlugin` integration; the existing build pipeline still pulls npm `lightningcss` until the WASM-vs-FFI decision matrix lands. |
| **`@gjsify/rolldown-native` (Vala + Rust cdylib)** | Phase D-2 POC bridge to the `rolldown` crate. Rust shim (`src/rust/`, path-dep on `refs/rolldown` due to crates.io 0.1.0 having broken transitive deps) wraps `rolldown::Bundler::generate()` in a per-call current-thread tokio runtime + JSON I/O at the FFI boundary; C glue (`src/vala/gjsify-rolldown-glue.c`) translates the malloc'd Rust JSON string into `GBytes` + `GError`; Vala wraps that as `GjsifyRolldown.Bundler.bundle()`. Used today only by future Phase B `gjsify build` engine swap; the existing pipeline still calls npm `rolldown` until plugin callbacks (Phase B Hard Item) ship via the Vala signal-bridge pattern from `@gjsify/webrtc-native`. |
| **`@gjsify/oxfmt-native` (Vala + Rust cdylib)** | `gjsify format` / `gjsify fix` under GJS (Node-free formatter). Rust shim (`src/rust/`, path-dep on `refs/oxc` @ `oxfmt_v0.54.0` — `oxc_formatter` is yanked on crates.io, in-tree only) exposes `gjsify_oxfmt_run(argv, argc)` (full pure-Rust oxfmt CLI in-process, panic-guarded via `catch_unwind`) + one-shot `gjsify_oxfmt_format()`; C glue (`src/vala/gjsify-oxfmt-glue.c`) translates the Rust structs into `GBytes` + `GError`; Vala wraps them as `GjsifyOxfmt.Formatter.run()` / `.format()`. Consumed by `cli/src/utils/oxc-resolve.ts`'s dual-engine pick. |
