**Fixed by PR #404** — `createZstdDecompress` / `createZstdCompress` stubs (plus the full Zstd callback + sync surface) are now exported from `@gjsify/zlib`. The module-init TypeError is gone.

### yjs (`tests/integration/yjs/`)

8 spec files ported from yjs@13.6.31 + y-protocols upstream tests into `@gjsify/unit` style. **Node: 147 assertions / 54 test cases green (~95 ms). GJS: 147 assertions / 54 test cases green (~80 ms), 0 skips.**

Yjs is the de-facto JavaScript CRDT — it backs [TipTap](https://tiptap.dev/), [ProseMirror](https://prosemirror.net/) collab, [BlockNote](https://www.blocknotejs.org/), [Hocuspocus](https://tiptap.dev/docs/hocuspocus/introduction), and a long tail of collaborative editors. Unlike the sibling Loro suite (Rust → WASM via wasm-bindgen), Yjs is pure JavaScript at its core, so it exercises a different cross-section of the GJS engine: heavy `Uint8Array` + `DataView` use for the binary wire format, `Map` / `Set` / `WeakMap` for the delete-set + transaction bookkeeping, `crypto.getRandomValues` (via `lib0/webcrypto`) for clientID generation, and the standard Yjs sync companion protocol `y-protocols/awareness`.

| Suite | Node | GJS | Exercises |
|---|---|---|---|
| y-text.spec.ts | ✅ | ✅ | `Y.Text` insert/delete/format, observer delta semantics (insert/retain/delete ops), `toDelta()`, unicode + UTF-16 indices, transactional coalescing of insert+delete pairs |
| y-array.spec.ts | ✅ | ✅ | `Y.Array` push/insert/delete/slice, `Y.Array.from()`, regression for yjs#297 search-marker length, observer firing on every mutation, `Symbol.iterator` over a list of nested `Y.Map`s |
| y-map.spec.ts | ✅ | ✅ | `Y.Map` set/get/has/delete/clear/size, `keys()` / `values()` / `entries()` iterators, nested `Y.Map` + `Y.Array` round-trip via `toJSON()`, observer `keysChanged` set per mutation |
| y-xml.spec.ts | ✅ | ✅ | `Y.XmlElement` setAttribute / hasAttribute / removeAttribute, `insertAfter()` sibling positioning, `nextSibling` / `prevSibling` linked-list traversal, `Y.XmlFragment` + `Y.XmlText` round-trip through `applyUpdate` |
| doc.spec.ts | ✅ | ✅ | `Y.Doc` `toJSON()` empty + recursive, transaction origins via `afterTransaction`, multi-op transaction coalescing into a single `'update'` event, clientID auto-reroll on collision, empty-id `getText('')` aliasing, subdocs `'subdocs'` event payload (`added` / `loaded` Set membership), `getSubdocs()` / `getSubdocGuids()` enumeration |
| updates-sync.spec.ts | ✅ | ✅ | `Y.encodeStateAsUpdate` / `Y.applyUpdate` / `Y.encodeStateVector` two-doc + three-doc convergence, state-vector diff being smaller than full update, `Y.encodeStateVectorFromUpdate`, `Y.mergeUpdates` of discrete update batches, mixed container-type round-trip, `Y.snapshot` + `Y.snapshotContainsUpdate` per the Yjs snapshot contract |
| undo-manager.spec.ts | ✅ | ✅ | `Y.UndoManager` empty-scope `addToScope` undo, global-scope doc-wide undo, double-undo, undo/redo on `Y.Map` including nested type restore, `stopCapturing()` discrete-step splitting, `stack-item-added` / `stack-item-popped` events, `trackedOrigins` filter |
| awareness.spec.ts | ✅ | ✅ | `y-protocols/awareness` Awareness setLocalState + encode/apply round-trip with `'change'` event added/updated/removed semantics, `removeAwarenessStates` explicit drop, encode preserves rich state JSON (cursor + tags), `encodeAwarenessUpdate` returns `Uint8Array`, `destroy()` clears local state |

Pillars exercised: `buffer` (Yjs encoder uses Uint8Array / DataView heavily), `events` (Yjs's `lib0/observable` EventEmitter shape — Observer pattern in every shared type), `crypto` (`crypto.getRandomValues` via `lib0/webcrypto` for `Y.Doc` clientID generation through `lib0/random.uuidv4`), `webassembly`-free path (pure JS — useful contrast to the Loro suite). No `@gjsify/*` source fixes were required to land this suite — the existing polyfill surface already covers Yjs's needs end-to-end on GJS.

The upstream tests use a heavyweight `TestConnector` simulating N concurrent users with delayed delivery + reconnect simulation, driven by `lib0/prng`. Multi-user scenarios in the upstream cases are reduced here to deterministic 2- or 3-doc sync via the canonical wire format every Yjs deployment actually uses (`Y.applyUpdate(b, Y.encodeStateAsUpdate(a))`) — same correctness assertion, no shared mutable test infra, no PRNG randomness in CI runs.

### nativescript (`tests/integration/nativescript/`)

On-device polyfill smoke suite — runs gjsify `nativescript:'polyfill'` packages on the **real NativeScript V8 runtime** (Android) and asserts pass/fail, closing the gap between *declaring* a `nativescript` slot and *executing* it. **14/14 green on NS V8** (NS CLI 9.0.6 / runtime 9.0.4, `@nativescript/core` 9.x, Vite 8.0.16): `@gjsify/path` 7/7 + `@gjsify/buffer` 7/7. Bundles the specs into a tiny NS app via the `@gjsify/nativescript-vite` composer, builds the APK, installs + launches on an emulator, and parses `__GJSIFY_NS__` markers out of `adb logcat` (a tiny local reporter, not `@gjsify/unit` — its `process.exit`/`system.exit` terminal path is a no-op on NS V8). Local-only like `autobahn/` (needs the NS CLI + an Android emulator) — **excluded from the root workspace** (`!tests/integration/nativescript`) so the heavy NS toolchain is not pulled by `gjsify install`, and not wired into CI.

Root-cause fix surfaced by this suite: **`@gjsify/buffer` constructed `TextEncoder`/`TextDecoder` at module-eval time** (`buffer-codec.ts`, `blob.ts`). On NS V8 those globals are registered *after* module evaluation, so the top-level `new TextEncoder()` threw and rejected the whole bundle's evaluation on app start. Now lazy-initialised (first-use) — no behaviour change on GJS / Node / browser (where they exist at eval time), Node 317 / GJS 326 buffer tests unchanged. The deterministic runner (`scripts/run-on-device.mjs`) does `ns prepare` → manual copy of the Vite bundle into the APK assets → `gradle assembleDebug` → install → launch, because NS CLI 9.0.6's Vite integration copies the bundle into the APK only in watch mode (see Open TODOs).

## Open TODOs

Tracked follow-up work that has been deliberately deferred. Every "out of scope" or "follow-up" note from a PR or implementation plan must end up here so future sessions can pick it up.

### `@gjsify/http2` lazy native-dispatcher loads still use a bare `require`

Two sites load the optional native HTTP/2 dispatcher through a bare
`require(...)` from ESM source — `src/client-session.ts` (`_setupNativeClient`,
reached from `connect()`) and `src/server/http2-server.ts`
(`_startNativeListen`, reached from `listen()`). This is the class documented in
AGENTS.md § CJS-ESM Interop → "Our source is ESM": the call resolves at build
time inside a bundle and is a `ReferenceError` from the unbundled `lib/` we
publish. Neither obvious fix applies as-is:

- a **static import** would pull `native-{client-,}dispatcher`'s static
  `gi://GLib` / `gi://Gio` / `@gjsify/http2-native` imports into EVERY http2
  consumer, defeating the optional-native-package design;
- **`await import()`** (the ESM way to lazy-load) requires making both call
  paths async, i.e. changing `connect()` / `listen()` — and Node's `listen()`
  contract is synchronous.

So it needs a real design decision inside `@gjsify/http2` (e.g. resolving the
dispatcher during an already-async phase, or an explicit async opt-in), not a
lint fix. Both sites carry an `oxlint-disable-next-line
typescript/no-require-imports` with the reason inline; they are the only
sanctioned disables of that rule in the tree.

### Manifest-conformance follow-ups

The five standalone declaration-vs-reality scripts are now one rule registry
(`@gjsify/manifest-conformance` + `scripts/manifest-conformance/`). Three
things were deliberately left out of that refactor so it stayed a refactor.

- **`gjsify manifest-check` is designed but not shipped.** The portable rules
  (`package-outputs`, `prebuild-artifacts`, `headless`, `field-coverage`) are
  already extracted into a package with a hand-written `lib/index.d.ts`, so the
  command is a thin wrapper over `selectRules({ scope: 'portable' })`. It was
  held back because it carries two costs a refactor must not smuggle in: the
  package has to flip from `private` to published, which needs the manual
  first-publish + Trusted-Publisher bootstrap BEFORE the next release train, and
  adding a command rebuilds `dist/{cli,affected}.gjs.mjs`, coupling the change to
  the committed-bundle gate. The name is settled: `manifest-check` — a sibling of
  `system-check` (machine has what the project needs) and distinct from `check`
  (types compile). Evidence it is worth doing: downstream consumers already
  declare `gjsify.storybook` (buchhaltung, pixel-rpg/map-editor) and
  `gjsify.prebuilds` (buchhaltung's ERiC package, which declares a prebuilds
  directory with NO `gjsify.platforms` — a hard failure in this repo, unchecked
  in theirs).
- **Five `gjsify.*` declaration kinds have no rule** and are deferred with a
  written reason in `scripts/manifest-conformance/unchecked-fields.mjs`, printed
  on every audit run. Four are judged unverifiable-by-construction
  (`defineFromPackageJson`, `flatpak`, `buildCache`, and `nativescriptPlatforms`
  until there is a per-platform artifact to compare against); the one remaining
  FINDING is `gjsify.storybook` (a typo in `stories` produces an empty component
  browser, not an error). `gjsify.main` and `gjsify.example` left the ledger when
  `package-outputs` claimed them: a declared `gjsify.example.node` must exist, and
  a declared non-gjs runtime must have the derived `dist/<name>.node.mjs` behind
  it — the shape `@gjsify/example-dom-excalibur-jelly-jumper@0.23.0` shipped.
- **The affected classifier does not know the new rule paths.**
  `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs` is a `GLOBAL_TRIGGER`; `scripts/manifest-conformance/**`
  and `packages/infra/manifest-conformance/**` are not. No coverage is lost today
  because `audit-runtimes.yml` carries no `paths` filter and runs on every PR, but
  the trigger list and the rule locations should be brought back into agreement.

### Toolchain hygiene follow-ups

- **Nine fixtures re-implement the prebuild-target name instead of importing it.** `resolvePrebuildDirName()` / `prebuildDirCandidates()` (`packages/infra/cli/src/utils/detect-native-packages.ts`) are pure functions of `(platform, arch, declaredPlatforms, existingDirs)` and are already the single source of truth for `prebuilds/<os>-<arch>/` — but every e2e that needs that directory composes the name itself, and several of them translated `process.arch` into the `uname -m` machine on the way. The `<os>-<arch>` unification had to fix all nine by hand, and one (`tests/e2e/self-host/run.mjs`) was missed on the first pass precisely because the composed string never appears as a literal: it was built from a bare `'x86_64'` in a ternary, so a grep for the path name found nothing and the GJS legs failed a whole layer away with "no usable bundler engine under GJS". Export a small test helper (or let fixtures import the CLI's built `lib/utils/detect-native-packages.js` directly, the way `tests/e2e/dlx-native-prebuilds` already imports `run-gjs.js`) so the name has exactly one definition, and delete the per-fixture copies. Until that lands, any change to the target vocabulary must be swept for BOTH shapes — the literal path AND the computed one.
- **`@gjsify/cli`'s `tsconfig.json` type-checks only what `src/index.ts` imports.** `files: ["src/index.ts"]` means `gjsify tsc --noEmit` never sees `src/affected-entry.ts` — the entry CI's `changes` job actually boots (`dist/affected.gjs.mjs` is bundled from it) — nor `src/test.mts`. A type error in either is caught only when the bundle build runs, i.e. in the pre-commit hook rather than in `check`. Widen to an `include` covering `src/**` (and confirm the emit stays `lib/**` only), or add the two entries to `files`.
- **`gjsify install` materialises EVERY platform package, so a cold install does ~4x the necessary work — this, not a wedge, is what the 30-min budget hits.** Measured on a fresh clone of this repo (linux-x64, warm tarball cache, 2026-07-28): 1597 packages / **4.78 GB** extracted, of which **183 packages / 3.36 GB (70% of the bytes)** declare an `os`/`cpu` that EXCLUDES the host, so npm/yarn/pnpm would never place them — six `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk-*` siblings at ~230 MB each, six `@pagefind/*`, plus the `@rolldown`/`@oxlint`/`@oxfmt`/`@img/sharp`/`@deltachat` binding sets. Those six 230 MB tarballs alone took ~22-45 s EACH to gunzip+parse+write under GJS, which is the whole "9/1597 after N minutes" shape agents keep reporting; the run reached 1372/1597 in 34 min while still making steady progress, and a re-run finished the remainder in 120 s (the `isAlreadyExtracted` skip path). The fix is to honour `os`/`cpu` like every other package manager, and it is a TWO-part change because `--immutable` materialises straight from `gjsify-lock.json`: record `os`/`cpu`/`libc` on lock entries at resolve time (npm's `package-lock.json` carries them for exactly this reason), and filter at materialisation. That is a lockfile-format change + a full `gjsify-lock.json` regeneration, so it wants its own PR + e2e; note the napi-rs entry-replacement in `napi-node-addon.ts` already selects its sibling BY HOST TRIPLE, so it is unaffected. Do NOT "fix" this by raising `--timeout`: a budget that exists to bound a hang must not be tuned to accommodate one.
- **`utils/stdin.ts` is the only reader of fd 0, but `@gjsify/fs` still has no numeric-descriptor support.** The CLI now routes stdin through `GioUnix.InputStream` under GJS (`readStdinText`), which fixes `gjsify affected --changed-from-stdin`. The underlying gap is in `@gjsify/fs`: `readFileSync(0)` / `readSync(0, …)` coerce the descriptor to the relative PATH `"0"` and throw `ENOENT`, so ANY bundled npm package doing the Node idiom breaks the same way, silently and off-target. Fix at the core (map 0/1/2 — and `openSync` handles — onto the Gio streams) and then let the CLI helper collapse back to `readFileSync(0)`.

### CI coverage follow-ups

- **`prebuilds.yml` covers every Linux target on a PR; `darwin-arm64` is still proven for the first time AFTER the merge.** The workflow now runs its BUILD legs on `pull_request` (same `paths`): native x64 + arm64 and the ppc64/s390x/riscv64 QEMU legs, Vala *and* the three Rust bridges — the break that motivated it (#827, duplicate `oxc_allocator` crate identity in `@gjsify/oxfmt-native`) was in the Rust dependency graph, so the once-proposed "Vala-only x86_64" shape would have been green on it. The emulated legs' cost was misreported: the "ppc64 3m00s / s390x 3m13s / riscv64 3m07s" figures once recorded here were taken while those legs were building x86-64 on the runner (`uraimo/run-on-arch-action` ignores its `arch` input when a custom `base_image` is given), so they timed no emulation at all. Under real qemu-user, measured locally on ppc64le with qemu 10.2.2: the dependency install is ~6 min (383 packages, weak deps off) and the seven Vala/GI packages compile in minutes; `@gjsify/lightningcss-native`'s Rust cdylib is the one expensive step. If a leg's total makes it the PR critical path, drop THAT package from the emulated legs rather than the architecture — its crate graph is portable and every PR already compiles it on x64 + arm64, whereas the Vala/GI half is both the part the architecture changes and the cheap part. `build-prebuilds-macos` remains the one PR-skipped leg (10x billing + the shared macOS concurrency pool); label a PR `ci:macos` to opt in, exactly as `main.yml` does. `prebuilds-summary` names the skipped legs per run, derived from `needs.<job>.result`. Closing the macOS gap permanently means either paying 10x per PR or adding a nightly full-matrix run. Pairs naturally with the "nothing byte-compares a committed prebuild against a CI-built one" item below.
- **`@gjsify/rolldown-native`'s darwin-arm64 leg is PROVEN but not promoted, so `gjsify build` still has no bundler engine on macOS.** As of run 30271998319 the manual-dispatch `build-prebuilds-macos-experimental` job builds the Rust cdylib + Vala bridge on a real Apple-silicon runner, stages BOTH libraries, passes `check-prebuild-loader-path.mjs`, loads under Homebrew `gjs` (`rolldown typelib loaded OK`) and resolves its sibling cdylib with `DYLD_*` unset — i.e. every gate the required job applies to `lightningcss-native`/`oxfmt-native`. What is missing is only the promotion: a build+collect+upload step in `build-prebuilds-macos`, `darwin-arm64` added to the package's `gjsify.platforms` (it declares `linux-x64`/`linux-arm64`, which is exactly why `stage-prebuild.mjs` refuses the target and the job hand-copies), and the download+commit wiring in `commit-prebuilds`. Until then the one job that covers the bundler engine on macOS runs on no automatic event.
- **`linux-ppc64`, `linux-s390x` and `linux-riscv64` have a working emulated BUILD again but no committed prebuild yet, on all eight bridges that declare them.** Two defects stacked: `uraimo/run-on-arch-action` ignored the architecture whenever a custom `base_image` was set (so the legs compiled x86-64 on the runner and staged it as ppc64/s390x/riscv64), and it also reset binfmt to `multiarch/qemu-user-static` (qemu 7.2), under which Fedora's package manager does not survive emulation at all. The job now registers a pinned current qemu (`docker/setup-qemu-action` + `tonistiigi/binfmt:qemu-v10.2.3`) and runs `.github/prebuild-toolchain/emulated-build.sh` in the target-arch image; verified locally under qemu 10.2.2 that s390x produces a real `ELF 64-bit MSB … IBM S/390` library and that the ppc64le dependency install completes. The mis-staged x86-64 artifacts were removed and each package records the gap in `gjsify.platformsUncommitted`, printed by `audit-runtimes --check` on every run. **A `commit-prebuilds` run on `main` is what closes it** — the audit then requires the `platformsUncommitted` entries to be deleted in the same change. Until then an exotic-arch consumer gets no native bridge and the guarded `imports.gi` probes degrade, which is the honest state rather than the previous unloadable one.
- **Fork PRs have no working CI at all.** Every container job pulls the PRIVATE `ghcr.io/gjsify/ci-fedora:<major>`, and a fork PR's `GITHUB_TOKEN` cannot read another repo's private package. Either publish the image publicly (it contains nothing secret — it is a Fedora + GNOME devel stack) or accept that external contributions cannot be validated until a maintainer pushes the branch.

### Cross-runtime reachability follow-ups (ADR 0014)

- **Nothing byte-compares a committed prebuild against a CI-built one.** `scripts/check-refs-pin.mjs` (wired into every `build:meson`) now catches the three ways a locally-built native artifact diverges from its pinned source — checkout drift, version skew against the npm engine, and a stale `build/` dir that ninja will not invalidate. What it cannot catch is a binary that was simply never rebuilt: the `rolldown-native` prebuild had drifted BEHIND its pin for an unknown number of commits and only surfaced when a rebuild finally happened. Close it by having `prebuilds.yml` rebuild and diff the committed artifact (or publish the CI-built one as the source of truth and stop committing hand-built binaries).
- **Three browser bundles are ledgered as NON-GATING in the new `browser` CI job.** The axis itself now runs (`main.yml` `browser` job: Playwright/Firefox on `ubuntu-latest` over the bundles the Fedora `build` job stages, 51 discovered, 48 gating-green), but `$BROWSER_PROBE_GREP` in `main.yml` carves out three that were red the moment it was first executed. (a) **`@gjsify/events`** and (b) **`@gjsify/util`** both declare `src/test.browser.mts` as `export * from './test.js'` — re-running the GJS/Node spec files in a browser, which AGENTS.md explicitly forbids. `events` HANGS (110 s Playwright timeout, `data-tests-done` never set); `util` dies on `process is not defined` in the `util.styleText` spec's bare `process.env.FORCE_COLOR` read (1 of 269 — the impl is fine, it guards with `globalThis.process?.stdout`; the spec is the browser-hostile part). (c) **`@gjsify/streams`** feeds STRING chunks into `new Response(stream).text()` in three cases; per the Fetch spec a body stream must yield `Uint8Array`, and Firefox enforces it ("Can't convert value to Uint8Array while consuming Body") where Chromium and undici are lenient (3 of 13) — a real engine difference, so the spec needs `TextEncoderStream` in front of the `Response`. **The same forbidden `export * from './test.js'` shape is in 11 packages** (`assert`, `async_hooks`, `buffer`, `constants`, `diagnostics_channel`, `events`, `path`, `querystring`, `string_decoder`, `sys`, `util`); the other nine pass only because their specs happen to be pure logic. Rewrite all 11 to browser-globals-only entries, then delete the ledger.
- **`@gjsify/worker_threads` ships a `src/browser.ts` with NO browser-axis test coverage.** It has no `index.browser.spec.ts` backing its `test.browser.mts`, so nothing ever asserted against that entry — which is how the exported `workerData` stayed permanently `null` (fixed now, found by reading rather than by a failing test). `@gjsify/zlib`, `@gjsify/vm` and now `@gjsify/http` show the pattern to copy. Worth doing before the package is considered for `partial` → `polyfill`, since export parity alone would have passed that bug.
- **`@gjsify/web-globals` declares `node: "polyfill"` but re-exports `@gjsify/webaudio`** (`node: "none"`, hard-bound: `gi://Gst?version=1.0` + a top-level `Gst.init(null)`) from `src/index.ts` and `src/register.ts`. A `--app node` bundle therefore hard-requires the external `@gjsify/node-gi` at module load. Fix by downgrading the slot to `partial` or adding a `src/node.ts` platform entry. Reported on every `audit-runtimes --check` run.
- **The ten `browser:"partial"` slots are RESOLVED as partial — the residual work is per-package, not a slot sweep.** All ten were audited against the `platform-entry-parity` gate; **none is promotable**, because in every case a NAMED export is unavailable on the browser platform itself, not merely unimplemented. The blocking export per package is recorded in that package's row in AGENTS.md's Node.js table (the authoritative location); the audit measurements behind that verdict:

  | pkg | value-export gap vs root | blocking export | promotable |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | crypto | 0 | `Hash.digest()` (`ENOTSUP_SYNC_DIGEST` — `subtle.digest` is async-only); `createDiffieHellman`/`publicEncrypt`/`X509Certificate` have no WebCrypto pendant | no |
  | dns | 0 | `resolve4` + every `resolve*`/`reverse` — no DNS resolver in a browser sandbox | no |
  | fs | **34** | `FSWatcher` never fires (no file-change notification API); plus the fd/`cp`/`opendir`/`glob`/`statfs` export gap | no |
  | http | 0 | `createServer`/`Server`/`ServerResponse` — no inbound TCP | no |
  | https | **2** | `TLSSocket`, `createSecureContext` (from `@gjsify/tls`, `browser:"none"`) — the UA terminates TLS below JS | no |
  | module | 0 | `createRequire` — the returned `require` always throws; no synchronous CJS loader | no |
  | sqlite | 0 | `DatabaseSync` throws from its constructor; `node:sqlite` is sync, OPFS `createSyncAccessHandle` is `[Exposed=DedicatedWorker]` | no (see below) |
  | worker_threads | 0 | `receiveMessageOnPort` — no synchronous `MessagePort` drain | no |
  | ws | 0 | `WebSocketServer` — no inbound WebSocket accept | no |
  | zlib | 0 | `gzipSync` + every `*Sync` — `CompressionStream` is Promise-only | no |

  Two corrections to the earlier reading of this item: **`zlib` is at 0, not 6** (that gap was closed by the `ZlibTransform`/`Unzip` work), and **eight of the ten already pass the parity gate** — so parity was never what was holding them back. Parity is necessary but not sufficient: it passes `sqlite`, whose `DatabaseSync`/`StatementSync` throw from their constructors. Treat a green `platform-entry-parity` as permission to look, not a mandate to promote; a routed entry must also have no unconditionally-throwing value export.

  Still open, per package (each is now a normal feature task, not a slot decision):
  - **`fs` — close the 34-export gap** over the existing in-memory `Volume` (`fstat`/`fsync`/`fdatasync`/`ftruncate`/`futimes`/`readv`/`writev`, `cp`/`cpSync`, `opendir`+`Dir`, `glob`, `statfs`, `openAsBlob`, `lchmod`/`lchown`/`lutimes`). Worth doing on its own merits; it does NOT unblock promotion while `FSWatcher` is a never-firing stub.
  - **`sqlite` — add a `./browser-worker` subpath declared `polyfill`** backed by OPFS `createSyncAccessHandle` (only reachable inside a DedicatedWorker), leaving `./browser` at `partial`. This is the only one of the ten with a design that could ever carry `polyfill`, and it needs a new subpath rather than a slot flip.
  - **`ws` has no `src/test.browser.mts`** — the only one of the ten without a browser leg. Its browser entry is 93 LOC and its client half is `globalThis.WebSocket`, so a small spec asserting the `WebSocketServer` ENOTSUP shape + the CJS-compat statics closes it.
  - **`@gjsify/crypto`'s root entry could become browser-clean.** Only 2 of its 25 root modules have a platform dependency (`GLib.Checksum` in `src/hash.ts`, the `imports.gi` fallback in `src/random.ts`). Replacing those makes the ROOT run in a browser with FULL synchronous Node semantics — which is the one path that would actually earn `polyfill` here — and retires the 1,774-LOC `src/browser/` duplicate.
- **The `native` runtime slot means two different things, and the NativeScript bridge packages use the wrong one.** The routing rule reads `native` as "the RUNTIME provides this API — resolve to `<pkg>/globals`", but `packages/nativescript-bridge/*` declare `nativescript: "native"` in the sense "this package IS the native implementation" (AGENTS.md documents that convention). None of them ships a `globals.mjs`, so all five (`@gjsify/native-fs-bridge`, `@gjsify/native-platform`, `@gjsify/adwaita-nativescript`, `@gjsify/devtools-nativescript`, `@gjsify/storybook-nativescript`) resolve to `@gjsify/empty` with a warn-once on ANY `--app nativescript` build that imports them BY NAME — a shipping bug, not a latent one. It is also what blocks `ALIASES_NODE_FOR_NATIVESCRIPT` from being composed through `withDerivedSlotRouting` like the browser table: composing it today would only change 4 of 122 entries and all four turn the deliberate `fs` → `@gjsify/native-fs-bridge` into `@gjsify/empty`. Fix by settling the vocabulary (either a new slot value for "this package is the runtime-native impl", or re-declaring the five as `polyfill`) — an ADR-sized decision because it changes a published `package.json#gjsify.runtimes` contract and `scripts/audit-runtimes.mjs`. Compose the NS table in the same change.
- **Rolldown 1.1.4 emits the `keepNames` helper AFTER its first use.** With `output.keepNames = true` (gjsify's default whenever `minify` is on) a minified bundle can contain `__name(fn, 'x')` at byte ~200 while `var p = Object.defineProperty, __name = (e,t) => …` appears ~9 kB later; `var` hoisting makes the early call `TypeError: __name is not a function`. Reproduced on `--app node` with the `@gjsify/module` node-gi test bundle, where the `\0gjsify-gi-node:*` virtual module is ordered first; `--minify false` emits no helper at all and runs. This is what `@gjsify/module` now hits under `scripts/node-gi-consumer-harness.mjs` after the alias-scoping fix removed the earlier `filename_from_uri` failure. Upstream (`refs/rolldown`, pinned `v1.1.4` in lockstep with `@gjsify/rolldown-native`) — needs a minimal reproducer filed, or a chunk-prelude workaround if the pin cannot move.

### `--app node` genuine-GJS-source detection is narrower than the reverse bridge it gates

`nodeGiGlobalsInject` now keys on BARE ambient globals (`print`/`imports`/`ARGV`) **plus** a static `@gjsify/node-gi/*` import in the tree-shaken output (`detectNodeGiModuleImports` — the externalised spelling of the bare `system`/`gettext`/`cairo` built-ins; such a bundle is already bridge-bound at load, so the signal cannot cost plain-Node loadability). That closes the portable-spelling half: a GJS source whose only host reach is `system.programArgs` (no ambient global) is recognised again (`gjsify storybook --runtime node` regressed exactly there when the storybook stopped referencing `imports`/`ARGV`). REMAINING narrowness: a genuine GJS source whose ONLY platform reach is `gi://` (no ambient global, no bare built-in, no explicit `--globals`) is still not recognised — its `@girs/*` value imports are emptied (`class extends undefined`) and its `/register` imports route to `@gjsify/empty`. A surviving `gi://` import CANNOT simply become the third signal: its shim loads node-gi LAZILY precisely so a gjs-gated `gi://` import keeps a cross-platform package's node bundle loadable on plain Node (#641, pinned by `tests/e2e/node-gi-globals-inject`'s gated case) — closing this needs a signal that separates an unconditionally-evaluated namespace use from a gated one, which the emitted-chunk scan cannot express today.

### `@gjsify/node-gi` — a pointer struct FIELD whose length lives in a sibling field marshals EMPTY

`GstMapInfo.data` is a `guint8*` field whose length is carried by the sibling `size` field — a dependency GI cannot express for a struct-field READ. gjs resolves it; node-gi returns an empty array, and reports no error while doing so. Measured on a decoded audio sample:

    map: ok=true  size=8192  data.length=0   buffer.get_size()=8192
    buffer.extract_dup(0, 8192) → 8192

That silent zero is what made audio inaudible on node for the whole of this investigation: `decodeAudioDataSync` pulled samples with correctly negotiated caps and copied nothing out of them, so every layer above reported success on an empty buffer. `@gjsify/webaudio` now uses the copying `gst_buffer_extract_dup` instead, which works on both runtimes — but any consumer reading a length-in-a-sibling-field pointer will hit this, and the empty result is indistinguishable from a genuinely empty buffer.

Fix shape: honour the GIR's `array length=` annotation on struct FIELDS in the field reader (the call-argument path already does), and where the annotation is absent, prefer failing loudly over returning an empty array.

### `@gjsify/node-gi` — `GTK_IS_EVENT_CONTROLLER` assertion failures on the reverse bridge

Running any GTK app through node-gi intermittently produces

    Gtk-CRITICAL **: gtk_event_controller_handle_crossing:
    assertion 'GTK_IS_EVENT_CONTROLLER (controller)' failed

and can take the process down mid-frame. NONDETERMINISTIC, which is the trap: single runs prove nothing in either direction. Measured on the showcase — node 1/6/1 criticals over three consecutive runs, bun likewise, deno clean in the same sample.

It is INDEPENDENT of audio, and that took a correction to establish: it first appeared right after audio started working on node, which read as "live GStreamer corrupts GTK objects". Re-measuring killed that theory — the criticals still occur with audio gated off, and with the GValue marshalling fix reverted entirely, i.e. on code that predates all of it. The event controllers are attached by `@gjsify/event-bridge` via `attachEventControllers`, so the likely shape is the JS wrapper for a controller being collected while GTK still holds the C object — a toggle-ref/lifetime question in the same family as the GC coupling below, not a GStreamer one.

### `@gjsify/node-gi` — the `$gtype` surface is incomplete

gjs exposes `$gtype` uniformly (`[object GType for 'X']`); node-gi does not, and the three shapes fail differently — measured against gjs on the same source:

| expression | gjs | node-gi |
|---|---|---|
| `Gio.ApplicationFlags.$gtype` | `GApplicationFlags` | `undefined` (`makeEnum` freezes a plain member object, no lazy getter) |
| `GLib.Variant.$gtype` | `GVariant` | a static-method THUNK — `$gtype` falls through the struct proxy to method resolution |
| `String(Gio.Application.$gtype)` | `[object GType for 'GApplication']` | throws `Cannot convert object to primitive value` (the GType handle is a bare tagged External) |

The handle works fine as an ARGUMENT (`GObject.Value.init(GObject.TYPE_STRING)` round-trips), so this is a surface gap, not a marshalling one — it bites code that reads a GType off a namespace rather than passing one through. Found while writing the `gvalue-in` conformance program, which had to route around all three.

Fix shape: attach the same lazy `$gtype` getter `defineLazyGType` gives classes to `makeEnum`'s frozen object and to the struct path that misses it, and give the GType handle a `toString`/`Symbol.toPrimitive` + `.name` so it prints like gjs's GType object.

### `@gjsify/napi` — a tsfn claim nobody hands back still leaks its control block

`finalize_env_tsfns` (`src/cc/tsfn.cc`) partitions `thread_count` by owner — claims a foreign thread demonstrably holds (`napi_acquire_threadsafe_function`, or the ABI-contract proof that a `napi_call_threadsafe_function` caller owns one), claims the JS thread holds, and `initial_thread_count` claims no thread has ever been observed to hold. Only the foreign bucket is joined (2 s deadline); the rest cannot drop while the JS thread is blocked in that join, so waiting on them was a deterministic stall and is gone. Whatever is still outstanding afterwards makes the tsfn DETACH — its JS-side resources are freed and the control block is handed to whichever thread returns the last claim, which then frees it. That is Node's `MaybeDelete()` posture (`refs/node/src/node_api.cc`: `if (thread_count > 0) { ReleaseResources(); return; }`) and it closes the force-free UAF window for good, but it inherits Node's consequence: **if no thread ever returns the claim, ~840 bytes leak for the process lifetime** (measured: 264 direct + 576 indirect, valgrind, 0 memory errors). Both outcomes warn unconditionally. Two residuals worth a decision later:

- An unattributed claim that a *foreign* thread genuinely holds — it never called before teardown, so nothing proved ownership — is not joined. It is still safe (that thread frees the struct when it does come back) and it still warns, but the warning has to say "never attributed" rather than name the owner. Closing this needs an ownership signal N-API does not expose; the addon hands `initial_thread_count` claims out through memory the shim never sees.
- Nothing reclaims a detached control block at process exit. A per-env registry of detached tsfns freed after the last claimant window would trade the leak for a much harder lifetime question; today the leak is accepted because Node accepts it.

Measured on every CI run by `test/tsfn-teardown-gate.mjs` (`gjsify run test:gate:tsfn-teardown`, Linux + macOS legs), which times the teardown of each claim-owner shape rather than asserting on a hang.

### Regenerate the register-globals closure map after a `GJS_GLOBALS_MAP` change

`node packages/infra/cli/scripts/generate-register-closure.mjs` (`--check` reports staleness). A stale map is fail-soft — builds stay correct but pay extra `--globals auto` analysis passes. (The related hazard — the committed CLI bundle inlining a stale map — is closed: `.githooks/pre-commit` triggers on `packages/infra/resolve-npm/lib/`, and on `packages/infra/rolldown-plugin-gjsify/src/` for the same reason: the bundle inlines the whole build pipeline, so a resolver fix appears to do nothing until it is rebuilt.)

### `@gjsify/rolldown-native` macOS prebuild — the last step to a Node-free toolchain on macOS

The Rust blocker is GONE (three `libc::eventfd(2)` descriptors → portable anonymous pipes in `src/rust/src/wakeup.rs`; `cargo check --target aarch64-apple-darwin` is green) and `meson.build` is darwin-ready — but no NATIVE macOS build exists yet: nothing has linked the `.dylib`, generated its typelib, or loaded it under Homebrew `gjs`. It is wired into `prebuilds.yml`'s manual-dispatch `build-prebuilds-macos-experimental` job (needs `json-glib` on top of that job's brew closure, `git submodule update --init refs/rolldown`, and a rustup toolchain), with a load-test step so a typelib recording the wrong shared-library leaf fails loudly instead of shipping dead. Run it, then promote the package into the REQUIRED `build-prebuilds-macos` job, add `darwin-arm64` to `package.json#gjsify.platforms`, and commit the prebuild. Until that leg is green the docs must keep describing the Node-free toolchain as Linux-only. The CLI-side loading follow-ups are DONE: `detectNativePackages()` no longer hardcodes a `linux-` prefix (it resolves `<os>-<arch>` for the running host, accepting both the uname-style spelling the Vala bridges stage and the node-style one `@gjsify/node-gi`/`@gjsify/napi` stage, preferring whatever `package.json#gjsify.platforms` declares), and `buildNativeEnv()` emits the loader variable the host actually reads — `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` on Linux, `DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH` on macOS, `PATH` (`;`-separated) on Windows. So the only thing still missing is the artifact itself.

### Follow-up — adwaita-web style isolation (ADR 0010)

The style-isolation boundary reset (`scss/_reset.scss`, PR #789) landed the fix — a host page's inherited typography no longer leaks into embedded light-DOM widgets. Remaining:

- Document the `--adw-*` / `--*` token set as the public theming contract on the website (it is now the sanctioned external-override API — the counterpart to the isolation).
- If a second light-DOM Adwaita renderer ever appears, lift the boundary reset into `@gjsify/adwaita-core` (headless) so both share it, instead of duplicating.
- Keep `$adw-components` in `_reset.scss` in sync with `src/elements/*` (`customElements.define('adw-…')`); guarded by `style-isolation.spec.ts`.
- Shadow DOM stays a documented FUTURE option (ADR 0010), not adopted — revisit only if embedding into hostile third-party pages becomes a hard requirement (open roots + `adoptedStyleSheets` + `::part` + Declarative Shadow DOM).

### Follow-up — adopt `@gjsify/adwaita-app` in the shell consumers (ADR 0009)

The package ships the native Adwaita app shell (runAsync lifecycle + devtools/CSS bootstrap, `createNavShell`, `loadIntoStack`/`LoadToken`, dialog/toast/file helpers, `readAppDevHooks`). Adoption is opportunistic, not a rewrite — wire each consumer onto it on its next shell touch, after the package is released:

- `@gjsify/storybook` — re-base `StorybookApplication` onto `AdwaitaApp` / `runAdwaitaApp`.
- buchhaltung (`app/src/frontends/desktop`) — replace its hand-rolled `application.ts` + nav split view + `views/util.ts` `loadIntoStack`/`LoadToken` + `toast.ts` + dialogs with the package (both currently consume the published `@gjsify/*`, so this follows the release train).
- eco-retrofit (`cli/src/app`) — same; this also fixes its latent `Adw.Application.run(null)` → `runAsync()` hang class.

### Follow-up — css-as-string bare-`@import` resolution (GJS CLI) + stale maker bundle

`@gjsify/devtools` exports `org.gjsify.Devtools` correctly in every app config — verified rigorously (real child object + interface present + `GetStatus` call) under `HANDLES_COMMAND_LINE` + an app-owned sibling object exported first, guarded by `tests/e2e/devtools-export`. The PixelRPG maker's "devtools absent" symptom was a STALE app bundle: `apps/maker-gjs/src/application.ts` calls `installDevtools(this)`, but the committed `apps/maker-gjs/org.pixelrpg.maker` predated that line (its `onStartup` goes `initControlInterface()` → `cleanupOrphanedPublishers()` with no devtools call; @gjsify/devtools code survives tree-shaking there only via the storybook's own `installDevtools`). `installDevtools` now logs a `[gjsify-devtools] exported …` confirmation so "did devtools come up?" is answerable from the app's stderr (its absence is the diagnostic).

~~Deferred blocker (why the stale bundle got committed): the maker fails to rebuild under the **global GJS CLI** — `gjsify-css-as-string` throws on a bare `@import "@pixelrpg/gjs/index.css"`.~~ **Resolved (gjsify core):** the real gap was the native backend, not the npm one — the npm path's `bundleAsync({resolver})` already resolved bare specifiers; the native `@gjsify/lightningcss-native` `bundle()` had no resolver hook (a JS callback can't cross the GI boundary). `css-as-string` now resolves + inlines every `@import` in JS via its shared `cssBundleResolver` before handing the flattened CSS to the native `transform()`, and an unresolvable `@import` fails the build LOUDLY with an actionable `[gjsify-css-as-string]` diagnostic instead of an opaque "plugin threw an error" (the native engine flattens JS error messages). Guarded by `tests/e2e/css-as-string-bare-import` (positive + negative, under the committed GJS bundle). Residual (map-editor repo, not gjsify): rebuild + recommit the maker bundle so its `installDevtools(this)` ships.

### Architecture backlog — ADRs 0001–0008 (accepted 2026-07-01)

Decisions in [docs/adr/](docs/adr/README.md), prioritized backlog + rationale in [docs/reports/2026-07-01-architecture-review.md](docs/reports/2026-07-01-architecture-review.md). Remaining work (strike per-item as it lands; delete this entry when all ADRs are implemented or superseded):

- **ADR 0001 (P1)** — install non-destructive invariant: ~~`tests/e2e/install-non-destructive` dirty-fixture guard~~ ✓ (4 modes: install / --immutable / add-package / workspace, all assert `git status --porcelain`) + ~~AGENTS.md invariant line~~ ✓ → ~~cross-process file lock for tarball store/global prefix (fixes the 5+-concurrent-install hang)~~ ✓ *(per-prefix lock `install-lock.ts` + atomic lockfile writes; the XDG tarball/packument stores were already atomic-rename and stay lock-free; `tests/e2e/install-concurrent`)* → ~~resolver conflict warning~~ ✓ *(`[gjsify] warning: version conflict …` names ranges, requesters and the picked version; `tests/e2e/install-conflict-warning`)* → Phase D.8 dedup pass.

Website: the tier model is documented on the versioning page; a per-package index is still pending.

### N-API host in GJS (`@gjsify/napi`) — Phase 0 + 1 complete (Tier 3, new axis)

The **inverse of Axis 5 at the native-ABI level**: node-gi runs GObject-Introspection code IN Node via Node's N-API; `@gjsify/napi` runs Node's **N-API `.node` addons IN GJS** by implementing the Node-API C ABI over GJS's SpiderMonkey (mozjs-140). Package `packages/napi/napi` → `@gjsify/napi`, tier 3, NOT a workspace member (own `napi.yml` CI; `packages/napi/**` in the affected-classifier IGNORE). Build = the `-native` template (meson + a thin Vala `GjsifyNapi.init()` GI surface + C++ napi-over-mozjs-140 in one `.so`+`.gir`+`.typelib`, loaded via `imports.gi`); bootstrap = the running `JSContext` (`gjs_context_get_current`/`get_native_context`) + a JSNative `loadAddon`; `napi_value` = a GC-traced `JS::Heap<JS::Value>` arena slot (moving GC). Consumers build UNMODIFIED with node-gyp (the shim self-promotes its `napi_*` symbols into global scope). ADR [0011](docs/adr/0011-napi-host-in-gjs.md) (Accepted). Full engineering plan out-of-repo (werkstatt no-dev-plans rule).

**Phase 0 status:** full `js_native_api.h` surface + module loader/version/fatal done; the async/tsfn/uv group intentionally loud-stubbed (deferred). First consumer **better-sqlite3 v13.0.1 (incl. its bundled SQLite) runs unmodified inside GJS, byte-identical to Node** — valgrind-clean. Conformance oracle (node's own `refs/node/test/js-native-api/*` addons, golden-diff vs Node): **13 pass byte-identical / 8 Phase-0-deferred ledgered / 0 fail**.

**Phase 1 status:** the threadsafe-function surface (`napi_{create,call,unref,release}_threadsafe_function` + `napi_make_callback`, over a thread-safe idle `GSource` on the env's `GMainContext`) is implemented, so **`@gjsify/node-gi` (the reverse-bridge engine) itself runs under the shim** — valgrind-clean under an 8-pthread flood racing forced GCs. The differential golden oracle (`gjs-napi` runtime in node-gi's `conformance.mjs`) shows node-gi-under-shim is **byte-identical to native GJS `gi://` across all 21 conformance programs (0 ledgered, no shim bugs)** — validating the shim against GJS itself *and* node-gi's Node/Bun/Deno goldens. **This is a CI test oracle, NOT a production path: on GJS, native `gi://` remains THE GObject path.** CI: the `p1` tsfn gate in `napi.yml`'s build-test job + a dedicated `nodegi-shim` job. The rest of the async group (async_work, callback scopes, `acquire`/`ref`/`get_context` tsfn, `napi_get_uv_event_loop`) stays loud-stubbed — node-gi never calls it and node-gi's gjs host-mode gates the uv path off.

Follow-ups (Phase 2+):

- **transparent `.node`→`loadAddon` resolver — SHIPPED (build-plugin integration).** `napiNodeAddonPlugin` (`packages/infra/rolldown-plugin-gjsify/src/plugins/napi-node-addon.ts`, the forward mirror of `gjsGiNodePlugin`) is always-on for `--app gjs`: it intercepts `bindings` / `node-gyp-build` / a direct `.node` import / a napi-rs `@scope/pkg-<triple>` sibling / a napi-rs **generated-loader entry** (`@node-rs/*`) and rewrites it to `loadAddon('<abs .node>')` (bare `@gjsify/napi`), auto-locating the compiled `.node` via node-gyp-build's own probe order (or the current-platform sibling for napi-rs). For a napi-rs generated loader it detects the native `main` (package.json `napi`/optionalDependencies signal) and replaces the whole module with `module.exports = loadAddon(<sibling .node>)`, so the `createRequire` loader body never reaches the bundle. So `import Database from 'better-sqlite3'` / `import { hash } from '@node-rs/argon2'` just works after `gjsify install @gjsify/napi` — no longer gate-scoped. Unit: `packages/infra/cli/src/napi-node-addon.spec.ts`; end-to-end (byte-vs-Node) via `napi.yml`: `packages/napi/napi/test/transparent-gate.mjs` + `test:addons:transparent` — all four byte-identical (bufferutil + utf-8-validate = node-gyp-build, sqlite3 = bindings, `@node-rs/argon2` = napi-rs entry-replacement).
- **implement the deferred non-experimental stubs** — `napi_*_bigint_words`, `node_api_create_external_string_{latin1,utf16}`, `napi_create_external_arraybuffer` (currently loud stubs → several of the 8 ledgered conformance programs).
- **crash-class hardening (deferred, non-blocking)** — null `state->wrap` via a back-pointer to close a theoretical teardown-finalizer sibling-unwrap UAF; Node-parity, not a graduation gate.
- **the 4 `NAPI_EXPERIMENTAL` conformance addons** — `node_api_post_finalizer` / `node_api_create_object_with_properties` / `node_api_is_sharedarraybuffer` (outside the Phase-0 `NAPI_VERSION=10` non-experimental surface).
- **node-gyp golden drift watch** — the node-gyp goldens were generated on Node 24 but CI runs Node 22; watch the first CI run for golden drift.
- **cross-platform prebuilds — macOS SHIPPED (incl. tsfn), Windows attempted+blocked (groundwork done).** UNLIKE `@gjsify/node-gi` (which links the portable girepository stack — Homebrew/gvsbuild ship it — so it runs on Linux/macOS/Windows), this shim links GJS's SpiderMonkey (**mozjs-140**) + libgjs, a far harder dependency. **macOS (darwin-arm64): viable + implemented** — Homebrew's `gjs` 1.88.x depends on `spidermonkey` 140.x (mozjs-140), the SAME pairing the Fedora job pins, so the intrinsic mozjs-major pin (ADR 0011) holds with no source build; the C++ loader is pure POSIX `dlfcn` (portable to dyld); `meson.build` branches the symbol-export map (ld64 `-exported_symbols_list` vs GNU `--version-script`) + the recorded shared-lib leaf (`.dylib` vs `.so`). The `macos` job in `napi.yml` brew-installs the toolchain, builds `libgjsifynapi.dylib` + `GjsifyNapi-1.0.{gir,typelib}`, runs the load + P0.x value-model gates **+ the P1 tsfn gate** on `gjs`, and stages/uploads `prebuilds/darwin-arm64/`. **The P1 tsfn gate is ENABLED on macOS (CI-validated only — the team is Linux-only locally). The earlier macOS-only exit-segfault (deliveries always passed — coalescing/flood-under-GC/blocking/abort — but `gjs` crashed at process EXIT) was NOT a generic GC-unload-order difference: it was a tsfn use-after-free. The tsfn was freed while foreign claimants still held claims — on BOTH the abort dispatch AND the env-teardown path (`test_threadsafe_function_shutdown`'s producer threads never release; they loop until `napi_closing`) — then a claimant locked its freed mutex / a scheduler hit its freed `GMainContext`. A UAF Linux/glibc tolerates (no-op mutex-destroy, freed bytes survive, valgrind's slow timing lets claimants finish) but macOS's allocator + exit-time dyld unload crash on. Fixed in `tsfn.cc` by never freeing under a live claim: the dispatch releases the tsfn only once `thread_count` drains to 0 (the tsfn analog of `async_work`'s `g_thread_pool_free(wait=TRUE)`), and env teardown joins the claims a FOREIGN thread demonstrably holds (2s deadline, per-claim owner attribution) and then DETACHES rather than force-freeing — the last claimant frees, which is Node's own `MaybeDelete()` posture. Linux stays clean: conformance 30/0 + P0.0–P0.4 + p1 + tsfn-teardown gates + all three valgrind legs 0 errors.** **Widening also deferred (not Linux-only, just a bigger first cut): the golden-diff conformance oracle, the better-sqlite3 consumer gate, the node-gi-under-shim oracle, and the valgrind memory legs (no maintained arm64-macOS valgrind).** **Windows (win32-x64): ATTEMPTED, blocked at gjs-on-Windows (blocker re-checked 2026-07-25, narrowed).** The shim-side portability is DONE and Linux-verified: `meson.build` now has a `windows` branch (a `.def` EXPORTS file — `src/cc/napi.def` — since MSVC `link.exe` takes no globs; the `napi_*`/`node_api_*` ABI already auto-exports via the vendored headers' `__declspec(dllexport)` and MSVC hides the rest by default, so the `.def` only adds the Vala GI surface; `gjsifynapi.dll` leaf recorded in the typelib; MSVC-syntax `/GR-` cpp_args), and `src/cc/module.cc`'s POSIX `dlfcn` loader is `#ifdef _WIN32`-ported to `LoadLibraryEx`/`GetProcAddress`/`GetModuleHandleEx`/`_fullpath` (the refactor is byte-behaviour-identical on Linux — P0.0/P0.1/P0.2 gates still pass). A manual-dispatch `windows` job in `napi.yml` (gated `workflow_dispatch` so it never reds normal CI) drives the from-source attempt on windows-latest. **What re-checking the blocker HARDER overturned:** a prebuilt MSVC-ABI mozjs-140 DOES now exist — **servo/mozjs** publishes `libmozjs-x86_64-pc-windows-msvc.tar.gz` at **`mozjs-sys-v140.13.0-0`** (SpiderMonkey ESR 140.13, the same mozjs major the Fedora/macOS jobs pin), overturning the prior "no prebuilt MSVC mozjs-140 exists anywhere" (vcpkg #7147 still no port; conda-forge spidermonkey is 91.13 + `skip: true # [win]`; MSYS2 #17734/#5699 still unmerged + mingw-ABI; gvsbuild still ships the GTK stack but NOT SpiderMonkey/gjs — all confirmed 2026-07-25). **The remaining WALL is libgjs itself:** no prebuilt libgjs exists for Windows (no gvsbuild gjs/spidermonkey module; GNOME/gjs upstream CI is Linux-only — no Windows artifact), and the servo/mozjs prebuilt is servo's PATCHED fork in a Rust-crate static-lib layout, NOT the pkg-config `mozjs-140.pc` + shared lib gjs's meson `dependency('mozjs-140')` consumes. So gjs must still be source-built (clang-cl, per `README.MSVC.md`) against a wired-up mozjs — the `windows` job walls at gjs's `meson setup`. **A SECOND wall waits behind it (specced, not yet solvable without a Windows host):** Windows has no POSIX global flat symbol namespace, so the shim's `ensure_napi_symbols_global` self-promotion has no analog — an UNMODIFIED node-gyp `.node` resolves its `napi_*` imports via node-gyp's delay-load hook against the HOST `.exe` (`GetModuleHandle(NULL)`), and `gjs.exe` does not export the napi ABI; binding unmodified addons needs a napi-exporting host EXE or a delay-load-hook cooperation (documented in `module.cc`'s `_WIN32` branch). **Unblocks when:** a prebuilt libgjs-win32 appears (a gvsbuild gjs/spidermonkey module or a GNOME gjs Windows CI artifact), OR gjs builds cleanly against the servo/mozjs prebuilt AND the delay-load host-binding is solved.

### GI/GObject runtime for Node (Axis 5) — GRADUATED Tier 3→2 (2026-07-14)

**Graduated per ADR 0005** — the four gate items landed (teardown crash #730, vfunc OUT/INOUT #732, GTK/Cairo layer #733, second consumer `@gjsify/sqlite` #734); `@gjsify/node-gi` is now Tier 2 (best-effort), with the dependency-isolation invariant retained (devDep-only seam). The headless core is complete and the GTK/Cairo layer landed; the GIMarshallingTests conformance oracle sits at 370 pass (0 fail, every skip reason-tagged). The GTK-bridge CAPSTONE is landed: Excalibur.js 0.32 boots + renders through `@gjsify/webgl`'s `WebGLBridge` on node-gi, byte-identical to `gjs -m` (`test/excalibur-webgl.test.mjs` + `fixtures/excalibur-webgl-app.ts`; the four core fixes it exposed — GVariant `'ay'` null, `hasMethod`/undefined unknown members, the signal-dispatch microtask checkpoint, the uv co-pump source priority below GDK redraw — are documented in the node-gi README). The roadmap below is kept for provenance; ongoing follow-ups are the `#### node-gi — deferred limitations` list.

Track (see AGENTS.md `## Strategic direction` → `### Axis 5 active track`): run **unchanged GJS/GObject-Introspection code under Node.js** — same source builds+runs on GJS and Node (`gjsify build --app {gjs,node}`); `gi://` + bare built-ins (`gettext`/`system`/`cairo`/`console`) + legacy `imports.*` all resolve to ONE GI backend; GJS overrides+globals carried over via the reverse of the detect→inject pipeline. Own native engine **vendored** in `packages/node-gi/*` (N-API port of `refs/node-gtk`'s `src/*.cc`, MIT + credits to romgrk; retargeted to **girepository-2.0** since standalone `girepository-1.0` is gone on GLib ≥ 2.80 — GJS's `gi/repo.cpp` is the modern-API reference). Milestone 1 = **headless core** (GLib+GObject+Gio): marshalling, the toggle-ref GC dance, mainloop (libuv-in-GLib `GSource`), `_promisify`, `registerClass`, gi://+`imports.*` resolution; GTK/Adw after. Roadmap PRs: ~~scaffold+minimal addon~~ ✓ → ~~marshalling~~ ✓ → ~~GObject/properties/GC~~ ✓ → ~~signals (connect/emit/disconnect)~~ ✓ → ~~instance methods (own + interface + parent-chain walk; GObject in/out + enum/flags marshalling)~~ ✓ → ~~registerClass step 1: subclass registration + construct-by-type (inherited props/methods via the dynamic type's ancestor walk)~~ ✓ → ~~registerClass step 2: custom properties + signals (class_init — GParamSpecs installed + custom get/set_property routing to a per-instance value store, owner-type distinguishing custom from inherited; `g_signal_newv` signals; detailed-signal connect `notify::prop` via `g_signal_parse_name`)~~ ✓ → ~~registerClass step 3a: vfunc override (a JS function overrides a parent GObject vfunc; an ffi closure is written into the class vtable at the class-struct FIELD offset — `gi_vfunc_info_get_offset` is GI_UNKNOWN/0xFFFF for GObject's own vfuncs, so the field offset is authoritative; the instance is passed as the JS `this`; per-class strong napi_ref + closure live for the process-permanent GType — same ownership model as the signal class-handler, NOT a per-instance cycle)~~ ✓ → ~~registerClass step 3b: vfunc chain-up + toggle-ref GC bridge (`super.vfunc_<name>(...)` invokes the parent vtable fn captured pre-override in class_init — the C default or a JS override up the chain — via `callParentVfunc` ffi_call'ing through the override's own cif; L1 wires `super.vfunc_x` through a chain-up Proxy beneath each introspected base class's prototype; the toggle-ref GC bridge landed earlier)~~ ✓ → ~~mainloop (libuv-in-GLib `GSource` nesting Node's loop inside the GLib loop; `startMainLoop` auto-attached by `requireGi`; boxed/struct slice — `GLib.MainLoop.new`→boxed handle→`.run()`/`.quit()`; promise-microtask drain has the documented nested-checkpoint caveat — L1 fix later)~~ ✓ → ~~L1 compat step 1: `requireGi` namespace wrapper (functions + GObject classes;
