Strike sub-steps as each lands; delete this entry when the headless core graduates.

**Cross-runtime (Node / Bun / Deno) — SHIPPED (#699).** The Node-API addon runs the GObject/GLib/Gio surface on **Bun and Deno** too, from ONE binary (no separate bindings, no Rust rewrite — Node-API is their common ABI). The toggle-ref GC drain moved from `uv_async_*` to `napi_threadsafe_function` (Deno exports no libuv symbols → `uv_unref` undefined; Bun panics on `uv_async_init`, oven-sh/bun#18546 — this is the first wall, armed by every GObject creation); the main loop gained a portable backend (`iterateMainContext`, pure `g_main_context_iteration`, pumped from a runtime timer via L1 `startMainContextPump`/`stopMainContextPump`) alongside the Node-only uv-nesting bridge (gated behind `RUNTIME`); `index.js` detects `RUNTIME` (bun/deno/node) + prefers a `prebuilds/<platform>-<arch>/node_gi.node` (Deno runs no postinstall build — `scripts/stage-prebuild.mjs` stages the ABI-stable binary). Node stays fully green — **396 node:test cases, 0 fail** (the --expose-gc GC-stress leg un-skips the 13 gc-gated ones; the display-gated GTK/WebGL/Excalibur suites run on the display legs); **Bun and Deno each run a 35-file conformance subset green** (`scripts/cross-runtime.mjs`, `test:bun`/`test:deno`, one process per file — Bun/Deno share a process across files unlike Node's pool; the Deno-2.1-era N-API quirks that excluded `arrays`/`async-error` are fixed as of Deno 2.9.x — re-validated per-file on 2.9.2, both files joined the subset). CI `cross-runtime` job in `node-gi.yml`. The authoritative full suite stays `npm test` on Node.

**GTK-GUI capstone (#57) — a real Adwaita WINDOW realizes + RENDERS on node-gi.** Beyond the display-free conformance + the WebGL/Excalibur GTK-bridge capstone: an unchanged `Adw.Application` + `Adw.ApplicationWindow` (HeaderBar/WindowTitle/StatusPage) realizes + rasterises a surface through the GSK renderer — `Gtk.WidgetPaintable` → `Gtk.Snapshot.to_node()` → `Gsk.Renderer.render_texture` → `Gdk.Texture.save_to_png_bytes`, the SAME path `@gjsify/devtools`' live DBus `Screenshot`/`DumpTree` uses (now proven under node-gi, not just GJS). Guarded by `test/windowing.test.mjs` (platform-aware display gate: implicit on win32/darwin, DISPLAY/WAYLAND on Linux; wired into `gtk-smoke` + the Windows windowing CI job) + the `showcases/gtk/node-gi-window` dual gjs+node showcase. The one core gap it exposed is fixed: **non-GObject GObject-fundamentals** (a `GskRenderNode` from `to_node()`, introspected as OBJECT_INFO but `gi_object_info_get_fundamental` — ref-counted via `gsk_render_node_ref/unref`, `G_IS_OBJECT` false) are wrapped through their introspected ref/unref (`MakeFundamentalHandle` + `isFundamentalHandle`/`wrapFundamental`, `src/object.cc`/`src/marshal.cc`) instead of `WrapGObject`, which had run the toggle-ref/qdata dance on a non-GObject → a `G_IS_OBJECT` critical cascade + a leaked ref. **Windows batteries-included windowing:** `@gjsify/gtk-runtime-win32-x64`'s `build-gtk-runtime.mjs --windowing` builds a SUPERSET of the display-free bundle carrying the runtime data a real GTK window needs (gdk-pixbuf loaders + `loaders.cache`, compiled GSettings schemas, Adwaita/hicolor icon themes + caches, Fontconfig); node-gi's loader (`gtk-runtime.js` `maybeWireGtkWindowingEnv`) wires the env off the `gschemas.compiled` marker (strict no-op for a display-free bundle). CI: `windows-gtk-windowing-runtime` (build) + `windows-gtk-windowing` (a real window realizes with the bundle as the ONLY GTK) in `node-gi.yml`. **Adwaita Storybook capstone (#57):** the FULL `@gjsify/storybook` Libadwaita component gallery (`showcases/gtk/adwaita-storybook`, 37 stories / 7 categories) built via `gjsify storybook --runtime node` realizes + renders on node-gi from the `--windowing` bundle as the ONLY GTK — driven headlessly IN-PROCESS (no DBus) by the env-gated `installStorybookProbe`/`GJSIFY_STORYBOOK_PROBE` (sidebar categories + chrome DumpTree + a representative story's `AdwSwitchRow` subtree + a non-empty GSK PNG, quit 0/1). One prebuilt `--app node` bundle proven on BOTH platforms: CI `storybook-node-gi-bundle` (build) → `gtk-smoke` (Linux parity, system GTK) + `windows-gtk-storybook` (Windows, no gvsbuild).

#### node-gi — deferred limitations (Axis 5)

Known gaps left for follow-up PRs (each surfaces a clear error or is benign; none is silently wrong):

- **Cross-runtime consumer survey — prioritized backlog.** `scripts/node-gi-consumer-harness.mjs` generalizes the `consumer-sqlite` proof (a package's OWN GJS suite runs `--app node` on node/bun/deno) into a reusable tool; the `consumer-suites` CI job (node-gi.yml) gates the proof set `sqlite`+`http2`+`zlib` under `--require-pass`. Full survey + gap report: `docs/reports/node-gi-consumer-survey.{md,json}`. **17 packages already run unchanged on node-gi** (sqlite/http2/tls/ws/dom-elements/node-globals/tty/**canvas2d-core (578/578 on node+bun+deno)**/**canvas2d (191/191 on node)**/**fs (657/657 on node)** + the pure-logic layers of devtools/devtools-browser/storybook/adwaita-app; + the pure-TS dual sample). Blockers to fix at the core, priority order (P1 no-mainloop async-drain, P2 bare-built-in externalisation AND the canvas2d-core marshalling residual — caller-allocates OUT structs for plain non-boxed C structs (PangoRectangle/`get_pixel_extents`, `BoxedHandle.rawOwned` g_free-owning field-readable handles) + JS `Uint8Array`→`GLib.Bytes` IN-args (fresh `g_bytes_new` copy, gjs `GBytesIn` release contract) — are ALL RESOLVED; conformance `caller-alloc-struct-out`/`bytes-in` byte-identical to gjs ×4 runtimes): **P3** — GLib/GObject marshalling-helper gaps (`ByteArray.fromGBytes`, `GLib.filename_from_uri` undefined; blocks `child_process`/`os`/`module`); **P4** — `normalizeEncoding`/`checkEncoding` unresolved when a polyfill is `--alias`ed onto Node (`crypto`/`string_decoder`). **P5 (harness prepends each consumed package's `prebuilds/` to `GI_TYPELIB_PATH`) is RESOLVED** — reuses the CLI `detectNativePackages`+`buildNativeEnv`, so `http`/`webrtc` now load their Vala bridge on node-gi (residual = downstream marshalling). Follow-ups: full 22-package `test:gjs-on-node` rollout + a non-gating full-survey CI job that publishes the table.
- **Bun/Deno conformance is a curated subset, not the full suite.** Excluded from `test:bun`/`test:deno`: the display/GTK tests (Xvfb; `interface-props`/`strv-construct`/`gtk-template*` re-validated green per-file on Deno 2.9.2 + a local display, so only the CI Xvfb leg keeps them out), the `--expose-gc` toggle-ref stress leg (Node's GC-safety gate), and the mainloop/runasync/pump **uv-integration** cases (they assert the Node-only libuv↔GLib bridges: the co-pump keeping the runtime loop live DURING a blocking GLib loop, and the uv-driven auto-pump draining GLib WITHOUT one — Bun/Deno pause their loop for a blocking app's lifetime, as GJS, and drive the non-blocking case via `startMainContextPump`; the `async-gio-await` conformance program is ledgered for bun/deno accordingly). The former **Deno-only N-API marshalling/async quirks** (`arrays` byte-array-with-autofilled-length, `async-error` `Gio._promisify` cases, found on Deno 2.1) are fixed upstream as of Deno 2.9.x — both files re-validated and moved INTO the subset.

- **Reverse-bridge polyfill routing over runtime natives** — on Node the global `fetch` stays the NATIVE undici one (the register convention never overrides an existing native), so `@excaliburjs/plugin-tiled`'s fetch-based fileLoader cannot load the root-relative `/res/…` asset paths that our GJS fetch/XHR resolve against the program dir. This is what blocks the FULL `excalibur-jelly-jumper` on `gjsify run --runtime node` — everything else boots (GTK window, devtools DBus export, `Gst.init` + per-Sound `AudioContext` construction, Excalibur into resource loading). Needs an opt-in GJS-parity-globals mode for reverse-bridge builds (route `fetch`/friends to the `@gjsify/*` polyfills over the runtime natives).
- **Gst audio decode/playback on node-gi — PROVEN on node, bun and deno; the runtime guard is REMOVED.** `@gjsify/webaudio`'s synchronous `decodebin` decode and its `appsrc → audioconvert → volume → autoaudiosink` playback both run through the reverse bridge on all three runtimes. `decodeAudioDataSync`/`GstPlayer` used to REFUSE outright whenever a `Bun`/`Deno` global was present, because a `decodebin` pipeline there nondeterministically SEGFAULTed the process; the cause was not the runtime but the `(transfer full)` GObject IN-arg ownership bug in `packages/node-gi/node-gi/src/marshal.cc` (the marshaller freed a GObject the callee owned), and with that fixed the crash is not reproducible. Measured on this tree: jelly-jumper on bun, 12 consecutive runs, every one surviving with a real PipeWire `float32le 2ch 44100Hz` sink-input owned by the bun pid, 0 crashes / 0 GStreamer-CRITICALs / 0 coredumps; a standalone decode+playback loop (144 decode→play→NULL cycles under forced GC pressure) clean on bun AND deno; the jelly-jumper decode set (3 WAV + 4 MP3) decoded correctly on node, bun and deno, plain, from inside a GLib dispatch, and with `startMainContextPump` running. Verifying against PipeWire rather than against the absence of an error is what makes this a measurement — a silent pipeline reports success at every layer (see the `GstMapInfo.data` entry above). The harness now gives a real verdict for this package: **node `pass 62/62`, bun/deno `partial 61/62`**. The ONE bun/deno failure is `onended` not firing in a BARE script, and it is the already-ledgered no-auto-pump property, not an audio defect: the `ended` event rides a `Gst.Bus` watch on the GLib main context, which node drains through the uv-driven auto-pump and bun/deno do not drive at all unless something iterates it. With the context advancing — a GTK app's blocking `Adw.Application.run()`, or an explicit `startMainContextPump()` — the same bus watch fires on bun and deno (measured: 12 decode→play→EOS→NULL cycles per run, `playback ended` every time). So `@gjsify/webaudio` inherits the bun/deno pump requirement from `@gjsify/node-gi`; deciding whether webaudio should drive the context itself (it cannot import node-gi — ADR 0005 forbids the hard dep) or whether bun/deno should gain node's auto-pump is a separate cut. Residual: no CI leg exercises any of this (needs a sound device), so the harness is the reproducible check.

  Its suite could not have told anyone: `src/test.mts` awaited the spec directly instead of routing through `@gjsify/unit`'s `run()`, and `run()` is what prints the summary AND sets the exit code — a broken assertion printed a red ❌ and `gjsify workspace @gjsify/webaudio test` still exited **0** (verified by breaking one on purpose), while the harness could only report `ran-no-summary`. Fixed in the same change; the same shape is worth checking on any package whose `test.mts` does not call `run()`.
- **`@gjsify/xmlhttprequest` — on DENO every XHR stalls at `readyState 3`, so an asset loader never completes.** Reproduced on the jelly-jumper showcase (`--app node`, `--runtime deno`): all 26 resource requests reach `open` → `send` → `readyState 2` → `readyState 3` within 10 ms and then NOTHING — zero `readyState 4`, zero `load` events, zero `error` events, zero `[xmlhttprequest]` warnings, for the whole 60 s of the run. Excalibur's loader therefore sits at 0 % forever and the game never renders. **Bun runs the identical bundle to completion**, so this is deno-specific, not a reverse-bridge property. Ruled out by measurement, so do not re-investigate these: GLib sources fire (a 1 s `GLib.timeout_add` heartbeat ticks for the whole run), microtasks DRAIN inside the blocking `Adw.Application.run()` (a promise queued from a GLib dispatch resolves on the same tick, and a microtask queued right after each `send()` ran for all 26), `Gio.File.load_contents_async` completes and its callback's own `.then` runs, and `GLib.filename_from_uri` + `GLib.file_get_contents` — the two primitives `readFileUrl()` is built from — return correct bytes on deno. The one behavioural difference found is that runtime `setTimeout` never fires during a blocking GLib loop on deno, but that is equally true on bun, which works. So the stall is inside `send()`'s `Promise.resolve().then(doFetch)` chain and needs instrumentation INSIDE `@gjsify/xmlhttprequest` (its `__GJSIFY_DEBUG_XHR` logs go through `console.log`, which the `--app node` bundle routes somewhere the terminal does not see — fixing that visibility is step one). Related but distinct from the reverse-bridge `fetch`-routing entry above, which is about the NODE target resolving root-relative paths against the native `fetch`.
- **vfunc chain-up — OUT/INOUT args now supported (#732)**; the remaining gap is **multi-level JS-override chains** (a registered subclass of a registered subclass), still rejected with a clear error — single-level override + chain-up to the C default (incl. OUT/INOUT args and caller-allocates) is supported. INOUT *container* args stay deferred (a catchable throw, like the function path).
- **struct/union field access + GParamSpec wrapping landed (#731)**; a `notify(pspec)` handler now receives a real wrapped `GObject.ParamSpec`. Remaining struct gaps: struct *construction* (`new Ns.Struct({…})`), array-of-struct-by-value element field reads, and GValue **BLOB** (byte-array) marshalling (surfaced by the sqlite consumer — a bound `Uint8Array` doesn't persist and a BLOB return comes back as a raw boxed handle).
- **`worker.terminate()` mid-native-call — the `Error::New` `SIGABRT` funnel is CLOSED; a pre-existing in-C-library `SIGSEGV` race remains (not node-gi-closable).** The napi funnel this entry tracked is fully resolved: a swallowed napi failure on the terminating env left a default-constructed (`_env == nullptr`) `Napi::Value`, and chaining the next fallible wrapper call onto it funnelled into node-addon-api's `Error::New(napi_get_last_error_info)` `NAPI_FATAL_IF_FAILED` sites (**outside** the `NODE_API_SWALLOW_UNTHROWABLE_EXCEPTIONS` valve) → `SIGABRT`. Every such chain across the call/wrap/marshalling/construction paths now checks the swallowed-failure residue (`IsEmpty()`) and bails cleanly: `MakeGObjectHandle`'s `External::New → TypeTag` (toggle.cc, both wrap paths), `MakeBoxedHandle`/`MakeGTypeHandle`/`MakeParamSpecHandle`, the `JsToGIArgument`/`JsToGValue`/element scalar+string coercions via the `common.h` terminate-safe helpers (`NodeGiTo{Bool,Int32,Uint32,Double,Utf8}` + guarded `JsValueTo{Int,Uint}64`), `ConstructGObject`'s `GetPropertyNames`→`Length`+name loop, `JsToGHashIn`'s `GetPropertyNames`, signal-connect/emit args, registerClass spec reads. Stress (child-process choreography, node v24.15.0): a hot method/static-call loop = **0 aborts / 200 terminates** (owner + non-owner env); a hot `newObject(...)`+props construction loop went **188 `SIGABRT` / 200 → 0 `SIGABRT`** (guarded by `test/worker-terminate.test.mjs`, both loop shapes). RESIDUAL — a lower-rate **`SIGSEGV` (12/200 ≈ 6%, IDENTICAL on the pre-fix parent build)** when the terminate lands while the worker OS thread is *inside a blocking GLib C call* (repro'd stack: `g_str_hash` ← `g_hash_table_lookup` ← `ffi_call` ← `gi_function_info_invoke` ← `InvokeFunctionInfo`, i.e. `lookup_action`'s hash walk — **no napi frame**). This is the terminating isolate/process teardown racing an OS thread stuck in native C code: node-gi has no hook once ffi has dispatched into GLib, it is pre-existing (not introduced by this fix — same 12/200 with the fix reverted), and it is the textbook "terminating a worker mid-native-call is documented-hazardous in Node generally" case. Not the `Error::New` graduation item (resolved) and not a graduation-gate blocker; closing it would need Node/V8 to quiesce in-flight native calls before freeing the worker isolate.

### child_process instant-exit pid — upstream GIO gap (issue #503; rewrite scoped + rejected)

`@gjsify/child_process`'s `spawn()`/`exec` read `child.pid` from `Gio.Subprocess.get_identifier()`, which returns `null` once GSubprocess's child-watch (GLib **worker thread** context) reaps the child — so an instant-exit child on a saturated runner can lose its pid (Node always reports one). **Resolved at the test layer** (deterministic alive-when-checked process) + **documented as an upstream GIO limitation** ("Upstream GJS Patch Candidates"). The `GLib.spawn_async_with_pipes_and_fds` + `DO_NOT_REAP_CHILD` rewrite (own the reaping → reliable pid) was scoped and **rejected for now**: it regresses `child.kill()` to a `/bin/kill` shell-out (no GLib kill-a-raw-pid in the GIR) and reimplements env/cwd/stdio/wait-status reaping on a critical path (execa/release-it/CLI) — disproportionate for an instant-exit-only edge. Revisit IF: (a) a real consumer needs a reliable pid for instant-exit children, or (b) upstream GIO exposes a spawn-time pid / preserves `get_identifier()` after reap (then no `kill()` regression). **Filed upstream 2026-06-13: [GNOME/glib#3981](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/glib/-/work_items/3981)** (pure-C reproducer + proposed `g_subprocess_get_initial_identifier()` accessor). **Maintainer verdict:** the accessor "would be OK" but is *not* prioritised — they steer toward pidfds ([GNOME/glib#1866](https://gitlab.gnome.org/GNOME/glib/-/issues/1866)) for process correlation, note the `null`-after-reap is *already documented*, and reject a construct-time `identifier` property (a property must match its getter). So the upstream accessor will not reach the Fedora-CI GLib for a long time, if ever: **the deterministic alive-process test + spawn-time capture is our stable, permanent posture, not a temporary workaround.** In the impl, the pid is captured on the statement immediately after `spawnv()` (`_capturePidAtSpawn` in `_spawnSubprocess`) — the narrowest possible window — and threaded through to `ChildProcess.pid`/`SpawnSyncResult.pid`; it stays best-effort for an instant-exit child (genuinely unobservable through this API) but the tests assert pid only against a still-running child, where it is reliable.

### `linkBins` is not `gjsify.bin`-aware — Node-less GJS consumer gets the wrong `.bin/gjsify`

`linkBins` (`install-backend-native.ts`) links an EXTERNAL package's bins into `node_modules/.bin/` from its npm `bin` field ONLY, never `gjsify.bin`. So a **consumer** project (no `@gjsify/cli` workspace) that depends on the published `@gjsify/cli` gets `.bin/gjsify → @gjsify/cli/lib/index.js` (the Node entry) instead of the GJS-first shim `writeWorkspaceBinShims`/`buildBinShim` emits (which prefers `dist/cli.gjs.mjs`). Harmless wherever Node exists (the Node entry runs fine); on a Node-less GJS host it points at the wrong interpreter — a silent-wrong-build for the Node-free promise. Fix: teach `linkBins` to read the extracted package's `gjsify.bin` from disk and emit a GJS-first shim (reuse `buildBinShim`) for those entries; also harden `mergeWorkspaceBins` against mixed string/object `bin`/`gjsify.bin` forms. (The install-HANG side of this coupling — a partial install leaving the wrong bin — is now moot: the extract-stall guard + hard-exit backstop make install fail-fast, see the AGENTS.md install "Hang/stall safety" invariant; and the version-skew warning surfaces the mismatched-CLI root cause.)

### `spawn(process.execPath, [cliBin, …])` under the GJS bundle (showcase.ts)

`showcase.ts:128` spawns `spawn(process.execPath, [cliBin, 'dlx', dlxSpec])` — the SAME `process.execPath`-is-the-bundle trap fixed in `spawnOxcLauncher` (`gjsify lint` under GJS re-executed the CLI bundle → hung). Under the committed GJS bundle `process.execPath` is `gjs`/the `.mjs`, not `node`, so `gjsify showcase <name>` under the GJS bundle spawns the wrong interpreter. Two-part fix (mirror `spawnOxcLauncher`): resolve the launcher via `nodeBinary()`, and — because the async spawn's exit event is not delivered back to JS under the bundle (`Gio.Subprocess wait_async` callback never fires → parent hangs at 0% CPU) — use `spawnSync` under GJS. See the AGENTS.md "Lint & format" note documenting both gotchas + the general "use spawnSync to await a subprocess from the GJS bundle" rule. The deeper root — `@gjsify/child_process` async spawn exit-event delivery under the bundle — is worth a dedicated fix so the async path works too (would also unblock spawn-based `gjsify test` under GJS); until then `spawnSync` is the reliable workaround.

### ~~BLOCKER — first-publish + Trusted Publisher for `@gjsify/oxfmt-native`~~ ✓ already published (v0.4.44+)

Package exists on npm since v0.4.44 (`npm view @gjsify/oxfmt-native versions` confirms). CI OIDC publishes it normally. No action needed.

### `@gjsify/sqlite` exec() compound-statement (CREATE TRIGGER) splitting

`DatabaseSync.prototype.exec()`'s `#splitStatements()` is now comment/quote-aware (skips `'…'`/`"…"`/`` `…` ``/`[ … ]` and `--`/`/* … */` when finding top-level `;`), but it is still a **token-level scanner, not a parser** — so a compound statement whose body carries inner statement-terminating semicolons is shattered: `CREATE TRIGGER t … BEGIN INSERT …; … END;` splits at the `;` after the inner `INSERT` instead of at the `;` after `END`, yielding an `incomplete input` error. node:sqlite gets this right because SQLite's real parser knows `BEGIN…END`. Confirmed pre-existing (the old string-literal-only splitter broke it identically) and out of scope for the comment-awareness fix. **Clean fix = let libgda's own statement tokenizer do the splitting** (option c) — currently blocked because `Gda.SqlParser.parse_string()` used iteratively hits a double-free under GJS and `parse_string_as_batch()` returns `Gda.Batch` objects rather than `Gda.Statement`s. A heuristic port of SQLite's `sqlite3_complete()` state machine (track `CREATE [TEMP] TRIGGER` → suppress `;` until `END;`) would cover the common trigger case but still mis-handle `CASE…END;` inside a body and adds real risk to the transaction-`BEGIN; … COMMIT;` path, so it was **not** taken. Revisit when the libgda `parse_string` iterative/batch limitation is resolved (then the hand-rolled splitter can be retired entirely).

### oxlint native path — deferred (JS-plugin host needs Node)

`gjsify lint` still spawns the npm `oxlint` Node launcher even under GJS. A `@gjsify/oxlint-native` GI bridge (mirroring `@gjsify/oxfmt-native` — oxlint's pure-Rust CLI core is wrappable the same way, and the `oxfmt_v0.54.0` refs/oxc pin even carries the matching `oxlint v1.69.0`) could only run the **Rust rule subset**: the JS-plugin host that executes `.oxlintrc.json` `jsPlugins` (the internal `gjsify/register-class-order` rule) lives in the Node launcher (`dist/cli.js`), so a native lint would silently skip that rule — a worse failure mode than requiring Node. Options when picked up: (a) native lint as an explicit opt-in subset (`GJSIFY_OXLINT=native`, warn when jsPlugins are configured); (b) port `register-class-order` to a Rust rule upstream; (c) wait for oxlint's plugin host to become embeddable without Node. Until then: `gjsify format`/`fix`'s oxfmt half is Node-free under GJS, `gjsify lint` (and the oxlint half of `fix`) needs Node.

### `@gjsify/oxfmt-native` arm64 prebuild + prebuilds.yml wiring

Only `prebuilds/linux-x64/` was built locally (Fedora 44, rustc 1.95.0, GLIBC 2.34+ — loads on Fedora 43+). Wire `packages/infra/oxfmt-native/src/**` into `.github/workflows/prebuilds.yml` (the rolldown-native precedent: auto-commit native prebuilds `[skip ci]`) so the arm64 prebuild is produced + future rebuilds are CI-automated. Requires the runner image to `git submodule update --init refs/oxc` + have meson/vala/cargo (same toolchain the rolldown-native job already installs, plus the refs/oxc checkout).

### ~~Native rolldown not loadable from a package-subdir cwd (Node-free `foreach build` under GJS)~~ ✓ done (2026-06-15)

`gjsify build` invoked under GJS with cwd = a workspace **package** dir (e.g. `packages/web/compression-streams`) can't load `@gjsify/rolldown-native`: only `resolveNpmPackage`'s caller-cwd anchor finds it (a package's `node_modules` carries only its own declared deps, NOT the optional-peer `@gjsify/rolldown-native` hoisted to the workspace root), and the workspace-root + bundle-URL fallback anchors miss under GJS (`createRequire`'s upward node_modules walk from `@gjsify/module` doesn't reach the root from a sub-package, and `findWorkspaceRoot` from a package dir doesn't surface the root either). So `shouldUseNative()` returns false → `loadNpmRolldown()` → `import('rolldown')` → the Rust N-API crate's `import('node:…')` throws `Unsupported URI scheme for importing: node`. Effect: a **fully** Node-free `gjs -m dist/cli.gjs.mjs foreach build:gjsify` (each child building from its package cwd) crashes; it only works today because CI builds libs via the **Node** CLI when `cli/lib/index.js` is cache-present (the `.bin/gjsify` wrapper short-circuits to `node lib/index.js`), or when the in-process `gjsify run` dispatch (the `feat/gjsify-run-in-process` branch) keeps native memoized from the root probe. Building from ROOT cwd works (caller-cwd anchor = root/node_modules). **Fix (option b, 2026-06-15):** `tryLoadNative()` in `bundler-pick.ts` now includes a raw `existsSync`-based parent-dir walk (`findRolldownNativeDir`) that probes `node_modules/@gjsify/rolldown-native/package.json` at each ancestor of both `process.cwd()` and the bundle dir — bypassing both `createRequire` polyfill limitations and `findWorkspaceRoot`'s `discoverWorkspaces` dependency. Fires only when the existing `resolveNpmPackage` anchors miss. 3 new unit tests in `bundler-pick.spec.ts`. Guarded indirectly by the cross-cwd-resolve concern in `tests/e2e/cli-bundle-cross-cwd-resolve` (which tests the resolver in isolation under Node, not the GJS sub-package path).

### ~~BLOCKER — first-publish + Trusted Publisher for `@gjsify/nativescript-vite`~~ ✓ already published (v0.4.36+)

Package exists on npm since v0.4.36. CI OIDC publishes it normally. No action needed.

### gjsify on Flatpak — SDK extension + fully Node-free self-build (roadmap)

Strategic direction (researched, agreed): ship gjsify to Flatpak so apps build without Node. The right primitive is an **SDK Extension**, NOT a custom runtime — gjsify-built GJS apps already run on `org.gnome.Platform` (ships GJS) with the polyfills bundled in as JS; a runtime would duplicate the Platform for no gain. An SDK extension is the idiomatic shape for a build toolchain (like `…Extension.node24`/`rust`/`llvm`).

- ~~**`org.freedesktop.Sdk.Extension.gjsify` SDK extension**~~ ✓ done (2026-06-08, `flatpak/`): packages the toolchain under `/usr/lib/sdk/gjsify` so a GJS app manifest does `sdk-extensions: [org.freedesktop.Sdk.Extension.gjsify]` + `append-path /usr/lib/sdk/gjsify/bin` + `gjsify build` at build time, with **no network and no Node**. **Named `org.freedesktop`, NOT `org.gnome`** — third-party SDK extensions live under the freedesktop `Extension` point (node24/vala/llvm precedent); it still mounts into `org.gnome.Sdk` builds because GNOME's SDK inherits that point, and `gjs` itself comes from `org.gnome.Sdk` (the extension is built against gjs-less `org.freedesktop.Sdk//24.08`, exactly as node24 is). Modelled 1:1 on node24's `build-extension: true` manifest. Ships: the committed `cli.gjs.mjs` in a `dist/` + the real `@gjsify/cli/package.json` (so `gjsify --version` is correct), the 3 `rolldown-plugin-gjsify` build shims (`console-gjs`/`unicorn-magic`/`module-resolve`, resolved bundle-relative — no node_modules), the two native-bridge **JS wrapper packages** (`@gjsify/{rolldown,lightningcss}-native`'s `lib/` + `package.json`) as a real `node_modules/@gjsify/` slice (the CLI dynamic-imports them via `createRequire`; their `gi://` typelibs resolve via `GI_TYPELIB_PATH`, set by a self-contained `bin/gjsify` wrapper), the typelibs/.so flat under `lib/`, and `enable.sh`. **Verified end-to-end (e2e `tests/e2e/flatpak-sdk-extension`, two tiers):** structural (every file source exists, build-extension shape) always, plus — when `flatpak-builder` + `org.freedesktop.Sdk//24.08` are present — a real `flatpak-builder` build whose payload runs `gjsify --version` → `0.7.2` and `gjsify build … --app gjs` → a real bundle using **native rolldown loaded from the extension's own node_modules** (proves npm-rolldown is not the path). The **consumer side already works** via the existing `gjsify flatpak init --sdk-extension …` (`deriveAppendPath` → `/usr/lib/sdk/gjsify/bin`) — no new `--kind` needed (an app *consumes* extensions; the *producer* is the single committed `flatpak/` manifest). Build/consume/distribution docs in `flatpak/README.md`. **Distribution deliberately left open** (per Flathub's Generative-AI policy, extensions/runtimes are explicitly in scope → a Flathub submission depends on the discretionary "mature, well-maintained" exception; a gjsify-owned OSTree remote sidesteps it). **Follow-ups done (2026-06-08):** (a) **`gjsify-tsc` in the extension** — ships `@gjsify/tsc` (bundle + 108 `lib*.d.ts` + package.json) as a node_modules slice + a `gjsify-tsc` bin; needed a small `tsc.ts` fix so `gjsify tsc` dual-anchors the `@gjsify/tsc/bundle` resolution at the **CLI bundle location** (`import.meta.url`), not only the consumer's workspace root — mirroring `bundler-pick.ts`'s native-bridge dual-anchor (a latent asymmetry: `gjsify build` already anchored at the bundle, `gjsify tsc` did not). e2e-verified: `gjsify tsc --version`→`Version 6.0.3` + a real `--noEmit` type-check inside the built extension. (b) **multi-arch prebuilds** — `x86_64` + `aarch64` (both committed) via `only-arches`-tagged flatpak sources (only the build's arch is fetched; JS payload is arch-independent). (c) **e2e pinned + CI-wired (2026-06-10)** — the suite was missing from `test:e2e` entirely (CI never ran the structural tier); now in the aggregate + per-suite script. Tier 1 asserts the `gjsify-tsc` wrapper + tsc bundle/lib manifest sources by name, the committed lib set is complete (≥100 `lib*.d.ts`), and the `TYPESCRIPT_VERSION` pin parses; tier 2 pins both `gjsify tsc --version` and the direct `gjsify-tsc` bundle exec to exactly the pin, asserts the built tree's lib count matches the committed set, and type-checks `--noEmit` against the extension layout. **Remaining:** a Flathub-grade offline-sources build (vendor via `gjsify flatpak sources` instead of `../` file paths) — only needed for an actual Flathub submission, which is itself AI-policy-gated.
- ~~**Node-free self-build (your "previous gjsify builds next gjsify")**~~ ✓ done (PR #483, v0.4.43+): the committed GJS bundle rebuilds the CLI itself (`gjs -m cli.gjs.mjs build src/index.ts --app gjs`, native rolldown, no Node — 0.02% size delta vs the Node build). Both `@gjsify/rolldown-native` bridge blockers fixed: (1) the C glue's `take_request_payload` folded "no entry" + "empty-but-present payload" into NULL → a `transform` on the virtual `\0rolldown/empty.js` threw "missing payload bytes"; now empty→empty `GBytes`. (2) the `unicorn-magic` shim resolved bundle-relative only → generalized `resolveShim(name)` with the exports-map `createRequire` fallback + a `./shims/unicorn-magic` subpath export. e2e guard `tests/e2e/self-host` ("GJS-CLI rebuilds ITSELF", `--globals none`). Remaining Node touchpoints for a FULLY Node-free self-build: oxc lint/format (need `@gjsify/oxlint`+`@gjsify/oxfmt` GJS bundles, mirror `@gjsify/tsc`) + switching the build-orchestrator entry from the Node CLI to `gjs -m cli.gjs.mjs`. yarn is already gone.

Modelled 1:1 on node24's `build-extension: true` manifest. ### Upstream PRs in flight (NativeScript) — track until merged

Two fixes we contributed upstream so NS apps work without gjsify-side workarounds. **Both OPEN as of 2026-06-04.** Revisit when either merges + ships in a NativeScript release: drop the corresponding workaround, then bump the version floor / re-validate.

| PR | Fixes | Our interim workaround (so we're not blocked) | Drop when merged + released |
|---|---|---|---|
| [NativeScript/NativeScript#11259](https://github.com/NativeScript/NativeScript/pull/11259) — `fix(vite): support Vite 8 / Rolldown` | `@nativescript/vite`'s function-replacement `resolve.alias` + `@rollup/plugin-commonjs` that Vite 8 / Rolldown reject | `@gjsify/nativescript-vite`'s `applyVite8Fixes()` drops both at compose time (the composer's whole reason to exist) | When `@nativescript/vite` ships Vite-8 support, `applyVite8Fixes` can shrink to (or drop) the two fixes — gate on the `@nativescript/vite` version |
| [NativeScript/nativescript-cli#6056](https://github.com/NativeScript/nativescript-cli/pull/6056) — `fix(bundler): copy the vite bundle to native in non-watch builds` | NS CLI copies the Vite bundle into the APK only in watch mode; `ns build` / `ns run --justlaunch` leave `assets/app` empty → SBG fails | `tests/integration/nativescript/scripts/run-on-device.mjs` does `ns prepare` → manual copy → `gradle assembleDebug` | When the fixed NS CLI ships, the runner can use plain `ns build` / `ns run --justlaunch` again |

Check status: `gh pr view 11259 --repo NativeScript/NativeScript --json state` / `gh pr view 6056 --repo NativeScript/nativescript-cli --json state`. No CLA is required on either repo (NativeScript's CONTRIBUTING has none; #11259's only extra gate is FOSSA "License Compliance", which passes). Both are auto-reviewed by **CodeRabbit** (an AI review bot) — address only any blocking findings; nitpicks are optional. Next step is a NativeScript maintainer review.

### Follow-ups for `@gjsify/nativescript-vite` (non-blocking)

- ~~**Register `xmlns`-referenced barrel modules (lift easy6502's `register-xml-modules.ts` workaround into the composer).**~~ ✓ done (2026-06-05): `gjsifyNativescript()`'s new `gjsify-nativescript-xmlns-barrels` plugin augments `@nativescript/vite`'s generated `virtual:ns-bundler-context` to `import * as` + `registerModule` the barrels an NS app references only via XML `xmlns="~/MOD"` (`~/widgets/index`, `~/mdx/index`). `ns-bundler-context` registers XML + paired code-behind + CSS but **not** standalone barrels (an `index.ts` with no `.xml` sibling), and an ESM bundle has no `global.require` fallback, so `loadModule(...)` returns `null` → `Module 'X' not found for element` (`@nativescript/webpack`'s `xml-namespace-loader` registered every `.ts`). Only barrels **without** an `.xml` sibling are added (the exact gap ns-bundler-context leaves); the modules enter the bundle via the added imports and register right after the upstream `registerBundlerModules`. Validated on-device: easy6502 `app-android` renders all views with the app-side `register-xml-modules.ts` disabled — that workaround can be deleted once app-android bumps to this release. e2e in `tests/e2e/ns-vite-fixes` (3 assertions). Lives in the `@gjsify/vite-plugin-gjsify` preset → covers both the composer and the `[nativescript(), ...gjsifyNativescript()]` path.
- ~~**Move the `css-tree` → bundled-dist alias into `gjsifyNativescript()`.**~~ ✓ done (2026-06-04): `gjsifyNativescript()` now aliases the bare `css-tree` specifier to css-tree's self-contained `dist/csstree.esm.js` (data inlined, no `createRequire`), resolved from the Vite config's `root` (css-tree is `@nativescript/core`'s transitive dep) and skipped when css-tree is absent. `@nativescript/core`'s CSS parser pulls css-tree, whose `data.js`/`data-patch.js`/`version.js` load data via `createRequire(import.meta.url)(...)` at eval time — dynamic requires Rolldown can't resolve, which then throw on NS V8 (`Module evaluation promise rejected`). The fix lives only in the Vite preset (the path that bundles `@nativescript/core`); the Rolldown `--app nativescript` factory pre-bundles gjsify code with the NS runtime external, so it never pulls css-tree. CI-safe e2e assertions in `tests/e2e/ns-vite-fixes` (alias present when css-tree is resolvable, absent + node-builtin aliases intact when not).

### NativeScript apps that pull web-API third-party deps — eval-time global injection (Welle 5 follow-up)

On-device teapot re-validation (2026-06-04, `three-geometry-teapot-nativescript`) confirmed the css-tree fix (the teapot bundle is `createRequire`-free and the APK builds) **and** that the showcase needs the same `"type": "module"` removal the smoke suite needed (its CommonJS Static Binding Generator broke otherwise — fixed). **But the teapot still does not render on NS V8 yet:** its third-party deps (`@nativescript/canvas-polyfill`, `@xmldom/xmldom`, `three`) instantiate **web globals at module-evaluation time** — `new TextEncoder()` / `new TextDecoder()` / `new XMLHttpRequest()` / `new FileReader()` at module top level — and NS V8 doesn't provide those globals that early (`TextEncoder` is registered by `@nativescript/core` *after* module eval; `XMLHttpRequest`/`FileReader` come from canvas-polyfill, which loads from the app entry, i.e. after `vendor.mjs`). So the bundle rejects on app start (`Module evaluation promise rejected: vendor.mjs`). This is the **same class as the `@gjsify/buffer` eager-`TextEncoder` bug, but in deps gjsify doesn't own.** The gjsify-side fix is a composer feature: **inject/seed the web-API globals (or hoist canvas-polyfill's registration) at the very top of the NS bundle**, before any module evaluates — analogous to the GJS `process-stub` `renderChunk` prepend. Design open (which globals, seed-from-`@gjsify/web-*` vs hoist canvas-polyfill, an `optimizeDeps`/`renderChunk` prepend). Until then, NS apps whose dependency graph instantiates web globals at eval time (canvas/WebGL/three.js stacks) build but crash on launch; headless logic packages (the smoke suite's `@gjsify/path` + `@gjsify/buffer`) run fine.
- **NS CLI 9.0.6 Vite bundle-copy is watch-mode-only.** `nativescript/lib/services/bundler/bundler-compiler-service.js` copies `.ns-vite-build/` into the APK's `assets/app/` only from the watch-mode IPC handler; `compileWithoutWatch` (used by `ns build` and `ns run --justlaunch`) never calls `copyViteBundleToNative`, so a non-watch Vite build produces an empty `assets/app/` and the Static Binding Generator fails. The smoke runner works around it (manual copy after `ns prepare`); the real fix is an **upstream NS-CLI PR** copying the bundle in the non-watch path too (or in `copyAppDirectory`). File alongside the pluggable-bundler PR (Level-3).
- ~~**Republish `@gjsify/nativescript-vite` clean.**~~ ✓ self-healed by v0.7.2 CI release — `npm view @gjsify/nativescript-vite@0.7.2 dependencies` confirms `"@gjsify/vite-plugin-gjsify": "^0.7.2"` (no `workspace:^`).
- **iOS smoke test.** Only Android was validated on-device (no macOS available). The platform path is symmetric (`.ios` extensions, `__IOS__`/`__APPLE__` defines) but unproven — add an iOS build smoke test when a macOS runner is available.
- **Conditional-export precedence.** `resolve.conditions` keep upstream's `browser` active alongside `nativescript`; a package with *divergent* `browser` vs `nativescript` conditional exports may resolve its `browser` variant. Decide a policy (drop `browser` for NS, or document) + add a regression test resolving such a package.
- **Worker builds.** The upstream `worker` config is passed through verbatim — gjsify transforms are not propagated into `worker.plugins`. Validate + propagate when a worker-using NS showcase lands.
- **Full ownership (Level 3).** The composer keeps `@nativescript/vite` as an optional peer. Owning the NS-runtime plugins outright (fork `main-entry`/`@NativeClass`/`cli-signal`, drop the peer) remains the larger goal — gated on whether the peer proves a maintenance burden + the upstream NS-CLI pluggable-`bundler` PR.

### ~~Medium priority — `@gjsify/fetch` streaming gzip decode (general fix)~~  ✓ fixed PR #519 (v0.7.2)

Buffer the full compressed body via `arrayBuffer()` before piping through `DecompressionStream` (same pattern `@gjsify/tar` uses for `.tgz`). Drops `compress: false` + manual `decodeJsonBody` gunzip from `@gjsify/npm-registry` (~60 lines). All fetch callers can now request gzip transparently. 81/81 GJS + 90/90 Node tests green.

Foundation (Welle 4-S refs + Welle 4-T tooling) lands the `--app nativescript` build target + `gjsifyNativescript()` Vite preset + quadruplet-aware audit. Per-package implementation is intentionally deferred for parallel agent execution. Order is rough (5-A is the smallest unlock; 5-F + 5-G depend on 5-A + 5-B):

Extend the suite per pillar as 5-B…5-D land. See *Integration Test Coverage → nativescript*.
- ~~**Welle 5-B — `@gjsify/fs` mit Native-Bridge**~~ ✓ done (2026-06-16): `packages/nativescript-bridge/fs/` (`@gjsify/native-fs-bridge`) ships `java.io.File` (Android) + `NSFileManager` (iOS) bridge. `@gjsify/fs` `runtimes.nativescript` updated `'none'` → `'polyfill'`. 7 tests (all pass on GJS + Node via synchronous guard). Pair with 5-E for on-device validation. See PR #525.
- ~~**Welle 5-C — `@gjsify/crypto` ohne `crypto.subtle`**~~ ✓ done (2026-06-16): `@noble/hashes`-based sync `createHash`/`createHmac` (7 algorithms), random reused from browser, ENOTSUP stubs for AES/DH/RSA. Package-level slot stays `nativescript: "none"` (main entry is GJS-only); use `@gjsify/crypto/nativescript` explicitly. `build` + `build:types` clean. See `src/nativescript/`, PR #526.
- **Welle 5-D — `@gjsify/stream` + `@gjsify/http`-client** (M): client-side over NS' native `fetch()`. Server-side (`createServer` etc.) throws ENOTSUP. `runtimes.nativescript: 'partial'`.
- **Welle 5-E — `packages/nativescript-bridge/` Native-Bridges** (M): new pillar. `@gjsify/native-fs-bridge` (wraps platform file API), `@gjsify/native-platform` (`Platform.isAndroid`/`isIOS` runtime detection). Mirror of GJS' `packages/framework/{webgl,canvas2d}`.
- **Welle 5-F — `tests/integration/nativescript/` integration suite** (M): set up `@nativescript/webpack` or `@nativescript/vite` consumer, run smoke tests via NS emulator/sim or Node-side stand-in (V8 + global injection of `java.*`/`NS*` shims). CI runner may need privileged container (NS emulator stack).
- **Welle 5-G — `gjsify create-app --template nativescript-*`** (M): mobile-app scaffold templates analogous to `template adw-canvas2d`. Adwaita-feel-on-mobile is an open design question — could use NS' native UI with gjsify polyfills for `@gjsify/fs`/`crypto`/`stream` providing the data layer.

Each Welle should be a separate PR + agent worktree at `/tmp/gjsify-ns-<welle>` to keep parallel work non-conflicting. Pre-requisite for all Welle-5 PRs: PR #420 (Welle 4-S refs) + this PR (Welle 4-T tooling) must be merged so the `nativescript` slot exists in the codebase.

### NativeScript build-feature ownership — adopting `@nativescript/vite`'s NS-specific features into gjsify's own bundler

Investigated 2026-06-03 (validated by porting `three-geometry-teapot`'s shared `start(canvas)` to NS-Android via `@nativescript/canvas` — the Utah teapot renders unmodified on the emulator). Strategy: progressively move the *generically-useful* NS-build features out of a dependency on `@nativescript/vite` into gjsify's own Rolldown/Vite plugin pipeline, in three escalating levels.

- ~~**Level 2 — platform file resolution + platform defines** (S)~~ ✓ done (2026-06-03): `platformResolvePlugin` (a Rolldown `resolveId` HOOK resolving `*.android`/`*.ios`/`*.native` source variants ahead of the base file) + `nativescriptPlatformDefines` (`__ANDROID__`/`__IOS__`/`__APPLE__`/`__VISIONOS__`/`__DEV__`), wired into both `app/nativescript.ts` (`--app nativescript`) and `gjsifyNativescript()`. Platform from `NATIVESCRIPT_PLATFORM` / `NATIVESCRIPT_BUNDLER_ENV`. Implemented as a plugin hook (not a `resolve.alias`) specifically because `@nativescript/vite`'s function-based platform alias is **rejected by Vite 8 / Rolldown** (`Failed to convert builtin plugin 'ViteAlias' … function replacement into rust type String`) — gjsify owns the feature and does it at the root. New `tests/e2e/ns-platform-resolve`.
- **Level 3 (north-star goal, L, multi-week) — gjsify as a first-class NS production bundler.** Make `gjsify build --app nativescript` (or the Vite preset) produce a *standalone NS-loadable* production bundle, replacing the need for `@nativescript/{webpack,vite}` for the JS-bundling step. The NS-runtime subset still to replicate (sizes from `@nativescript/vite`'s helpers): **main-entry + bundle-emit** to NS's expected dist layout (the runtime seeds platform globals → loads `@nativescript/core` globals → runs the app entry; `main-entry.js` ≈ 150 LOC sans HMR), **static-copy** of `App_Resources`/fonts/assets (S), **`@NativeClass()` transform** (`nativeclass-transformer-plugin.js`, ≈ 43 LOC), and optionally **app-components/XML page registration** (`app-components.js` ≈ 278 LOC — skippable for code-only canvas/three.js apps) + **CSS/theme-core** (skippable for non-theme apps). HMR is the bulk of `@nativescript/vite`'s complexity and is **not needed to ship** (production-only target). **Hard blocker:** NS's CLI bundler dispatch (`nativescript/lib/services/bundler/bundler-compiler-service.js` `getBundlerExecutablePath`) is a **hardcoded string switch** — `BundlerType = "webpack" | "rspack" | "vite"`; an unknown `bundler` name falls back to webpack. So `bundler: 'gjsify'` needs an **upstream NS-CLI PR** for a pluggable bundler (plausible — they just added rspack + vite), OR gjsify masquerades under the `vite` name (current Level-1 path). The contract is discoverable: NS spawns `node <bundler>/bin build --config=<bundlerConfigPath> -- <args>`, passes `NATIVESCRIPT_BUNDLER_ENV` (JSON env: platform, appPath), the bundler writes its dist, NS copies it into the APK assets (`copyViteBundleToNative`). Aligns with the Node-free / gjsify-as-build-tool direction. Until then, Level 1 (`bundler: 'vite'` + `gjsifyNativescript()`) ships via `@nativescript/webpack` for production.
